    11→## Problem
    12→
    13→Someguy v0.11.1 (latest release) has completely non-functional IPNS endpoints on staging. The `/routing/v1/providers/` endpoint works fine (returns results instantly), but `/routing/v1/ipns/` hangs indefinitely on both PUT and GET — curl times out after 30s with zero bytes received.
    14→
    15→This causes every delegated routing publish to fail after 3 retries x 10s timeout. The API falls back to DB cache for resolves, but publishes waste ~30s per attempt. Nearly 2,000 abort errors were observed in a 10-minute window during load testing.
    16→
    17→Staging has been reverted to `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` (PR #322) and someguy removed from the compose stack.
    18→
    19→## Solution
    20→
    21→Reproduce and diagnose locally using the Docker host (192.168.133.114) with Kubo v0.40.0:
    22→
    23→1. Add someguy v0.11.1 to `docker/docker-compose.yml`
    24→2. Point API `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` at `http://192.168.133.114:<port>`
    25→3. Test IPNS endpoint directly: `curl --max-time 30 http://<someguy>/routing/v1/ipns/<key>`
    26→4. If it hangs locally too — check someguy GitHub issues for known IPNS bugs, file one if needed
    27→5. If it works locally — investigate staging-specific factors (networking, resource constraints, DNS)
    28→6. Run SDK E2E (83 tests) and load tests to validate end-to-end
    29→
    30→Key questions:
    31→
    32→- Is this a known someguy bug?
    33→- Does Kubo v0.40.0 (vs v0.34.0 on staging when someguy was deployed) change anything?
    34→- Is someguy's DHT mode (`standard` vs `accelerated`) a factor?
    35→- Are there env vars or config we're missing?
    36→

## Investigate someguy IPNS routing failure locally

**Area:** infra
**Created:** 2026-03-23 (<1 day ago)
**Files:**
- `docker/docker-compose.yml`
- `docker/docker-compose.staging.yml`
- `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.ts`

### Problem
Someguy v0.11.1 IPNS endpoints hang indefinitely on staging — `/routing/v1/ipns/` never responds for both PUT and GET, while `/routing/v1/providers/` works fine. Publishes waste ~30s per attempt (3 retries x 10s timeout), with ~2,000 abort errors in 10 minutes of load testing. Staging reverted to `delegated-ipfs.dev` in PR #322.

### Solution
Reproduce locally with Docker host (192.168.133.114) + Kubo v0.40.0, test IPNS endpoints directly, check for known someguy bugs, and validate with SDK E2E + load tests.

The milestone establishes performance baselines before making changes, replaces the unreliable delegated-ipfs.dev dependency with self-hosted IPNS resolution, migrates rootFolderKey to an IPFS vault blob (achieving true zero-knowledge server), adds BYO-IPFS node support for data sovereignty, and completes performance baselines with client-side instrumentation and load testing after all features are stable.

     1→import { Injectable, HttpException, HttpStatus, Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
     2→import { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';
     3→
     4→/** Typed error for non-retryable HTTP responses from the delegated routing API. */
     5→class DelegatedRoutingHttpError extends Error {
     6→  constructor(public readonly status: number) {
     7→    super(`Delegated routing returned ${status}`);
     8→    this.name = 'DelegatedRoutingHttpError';
     9→  }
    10→}
    11→
    12→@Injectable()
    13→export class DelegatedRoutingClient {
    14→  private readonly logger = new Logger(DelegatedRoutingClient.name);
    15→  private readonly delegatedRoutingUrl: string;
    16→  private readonly maxRetries = 3;
    17→  private readonly baseDelayMs = 1000;
    18→  private readonly maxRetryDelayMs = 30_000;
    19→  private readonly requestTimeoutMs = 10_000;
    20→
    21→  constructor(private readonly configService: ConfigService) {
    22→    this.delegatedRoutingUrl = this.configService.get<string>(
    23→      'DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL',
    24→      'https://delegated-ipfs.dev'
    25→    );
    26→  }
    27→
    28→  /**
    29→   * Publish an IPNS record via the delegated routing PUT API with exponential backoff retry.
    30→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on persistent failures.
    31→   */
    32→  async publish(ipnsName: string, recordBytes: Uint8Array): Promise<void> {
    33→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`;
    34→
    35→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
    36→
    37→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
    38→      try {
    39→        const { ok, status, retryAfter, errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(
    40→          url,
    41→          {
    42→            method: 'PUT',
    43→            headers: {
    44→              'Content-Type': 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
    45→            },
    46→            body: recordBytes as unknown as BodyInit,
    47→          },
    48→          async (response) => ({
    49→            ok: response.ok,
    50→            status: response.status,
    51→            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),
    52→            errorText: !response.ok && response.status !== 429 ? await response.text() : '',
    53→          })
    54→        );
    55→
    56→        if (ok) {
    57→          this.logger.log(`IPNS record published successfully for ${ipnsName}`);
    58→          return;
    59→        }
    60→
    61→        // Handle rate limiting
    62→        if (status === 429) {
    63→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
    64→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
    65→
    66→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS publish, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
    67→            await this.delay(delayMs);
    68→          }
    69→          continue;
    70→        }
    71→
    72→        // Non-retryable HTTP error
    73→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
    74→        this.logger.error(`Delegated routing returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`);
    75→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);
    76→      } catch (error) {
    77→        // Non-retryable HTTP error — surface immediately
    78→        if (error instanceof DelegatedRoutingHttpError) {
    79→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
    80→          throw new HttpException(
    81→            'Failed to publish IPNS record to routing network',
    82→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
    83→          );
    84→        }
    85→
    86→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
    87→
    88→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
    89→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
    90→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
    91→          this.logger.warn(
    92→            `IPNS publish attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
    93→          );
    94→          await this.delay(delayMs);
    95→        }
    96→      }
    97→    }
    98→
    99→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   100→    this.logger.error(
   101→      `Failed to publish IPNS record after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`
   102→    );
   103→    throw new HttpException(
   104→      'Failed to publish IPNS record to routing network after multiple attempts',
   105→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   106→    );
   107→  }
   108→
   109→  /**
   110→   * Resolve an IPNS name via the delegated routing GET API with retries.
   111→   * Returns the raw record bytes, or null if the IPNS name is not found (404).
   112→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on routing failures.
   113→   */
   114→  async resolve(ipnsName: string): Promise<Uint8Array | null> {
   115→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`;
   116→
   117→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
   118→
   119→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
   120→      try {
   121→        const { ok, status, retryAfter, data, errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(
   122→          url,
   123→          {
   124→            method: 'GET',
   125→            headers: {
   126→              Accept: 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
   127→            },
   128→          },
   129→          async (response) => ({
   130→            ok: response.ok,
   131→            status: response.status,
   132→            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),
   133→            data: response.ok ? new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer()) : null,
   134→            errorText:
   135→              !response.ok && response.status !== 404 && response.status !== 429
   136→                ? await response.text()
   137→                : '',
   138→          })
   139→        );
   140→
   141→        // 404 means IPNS name not found - not an error
   142→        if (status === 404) {
   143→          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name not found: ${ipnsName}`);
   144→          return null;
   145→        }
   146→
   147→        if (ok) {
   148→          return data;
   149→        }
   150→
   151→        // Handle rate limiting
   152→        if (status === 429) {
   153→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   154→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
   155→
   156→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS resolve, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
   157→            await this.delay(delayMs);
   158→          }
   159→          continue;
   160→        }
   161→
   162→        // Non-retryable error
   163→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
   164→        this.logger.error(
   165→          `Delegated routing resolution returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`
   166→        );
   167→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);
   168→      } catch (error) {
   169→        // Re-throw HttpException immediately (e.g., parsing errors) - don't retry
   170→        if (error instanceof HttpException) {
   171→          throw error;
   172→        }
   173→
   174→        // Non-retryable HTTP error — surface immediately
   175→        if (error instanceof DelegatedRoutingHttpError) {
   176→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
   177→          throw new HttpException(
   178→            'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network',
   179→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   180→          );
   181→        }
   182→
   183→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
   184→
   185→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
   186→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   187→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   188→          this.logger.warn(
   189→            `IPNS resolve attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
   190→          );
   191→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   192→        }
   193→      }
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   197→    this.logger.error(
   198→      `Failed to resolve IPNS name after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`
   199→    );
   200→    throw new HttpException(
   201→      'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network after multiple attempts',
   202→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   203→    );
   204→  }
   205→
   206→  private async fetchWithTimeout<T>(
   207→    url: string,
   208→    init: RequestInit,
   209→    consume: (response: Response) => Promise<T>
   210→  ): Promise<T> {
   211→    const controller = new AbortController();
   212→    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), this.requestTimeoutMs);
   213→    try {
   214→      const response = await fetch(url, { ...init, signal: controller.signal });
   215→      return await consume(response);
   216→    } finally {
   217→      clearTimeout(timeout);
   218→    }
   219→  }
   220→
   221→  private getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter: string | null, attempt: number): number {
   222→    const fallback = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   223→    if (!retryAfter) return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   224→
   225→    const seconds = Number(retryAfter);
   226→    if (Number.isFinite(seconds) && seconds >= 0) {
   227→      return Math.min(seconds * 1000, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   228→    }
   229→
   230→    const retryAt = Date.parse(retryAfter);
   231→    if (!Number.isNaN(retryAt)) {
   232→      return Math.min(Math.max(0, retryAt - Date.now()), this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   233→    }
   234→
   235→    return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   236→  }
   237→
   238→  private delay(ms: number): Promise<void> {
   239→    return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
   240→  }
   241→}
   242→
```

## Summary: Someguy Docker Configuration History

Based on my search of the git history, planning documents, and codebase, here is the complete someguy configuration that was previously used:

### **Exact Someguy Service Definition (Last Configuration)**

From commit `c1c96de30` (feat: IPNS resolution improvement with Someguy sidecar and latency metrics), the someguy service was defined in `docker/docker-compose.staging.yml` as:

```yaml
someguy:
  image: ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1
  restart: unless-stopped
  environment:
    SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS: 0.0.0.0:8190
    SOMEGUY_DHT: standard
    SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_LOW: 50
    SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_HIGH: 300
    SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY: 512MB
    GOLOG_LOG_LEVEL: info
    GOLOG_LOG_FMT: json
  logging: *default-logging
  healthcheck:
    test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'wget -qO- http://localhost:8190/version || exit 1']
    interval: 10s
    timeout: 5s
    retries: 10
    start_period: 30s
  deploy:
    resources:
      limits:
        memory: 768M
        cpus: '0.5'
```

### **Image Details**
- **Image:** `ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1`
- **Port:** `8190` (internal to Docker network, NOT exposed to host)
- **Listen Address:** `0.0.0.0:8190` (required for inter-container Docker networking)

### **Environment Variables**
| Variable | Value | Purpose |
|----------|-------|---------|
| `SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS` | `0.0.0.0:8190` | Listen on all interfaces at port 8190 |
| `SOMEGUY_DHT` | `standard` | Use standard DHT mode (not accelerated, to avoid excessive resource consumption) |
| `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_LOW` | `50` | Min peer connections |
| `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_HIGH` | `300` | Max peer connections |
| `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY` | `512MB` | Memory limit for libp2p |
| `GOLOG_LOG_LEVEL` | `info` | Logging level |
| `GOLOG_LOG_FMT` | `json` | JSON log format for structured logging |

### **Health Check**
- **Endpoint:** `http://localhost:8190/version` (Note: Someguy has NO `/health` endpoint)
- **Interval:** 10s
- **Timeout:** 5s
- **Retries:** 10
- **Start Period:** 30s

### **Resource Limits**
- **Memory:** 768M
- **CPU:** 0.5 cores

### **API Configuration**
In `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml`, the DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL was set to:
```
DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=http://someguy:8190
```

### **Metrics Scraping (Alloy Configuration)**
From `docker/alloy-config.river`, someguy metrics were scraped via:
```river
prometheus.scrape "someguy" {
  targets = [{
    __address__ = "someguy:8190",
  }]
  metrics_path = "/debug/metrics/prometheus"
  scrape_interval = "30s"

  forward_to = [prometheus.remote_write.grafana_cloud.receiver]
}
```

### **Why It Was Removed**

Commit `796bbd2b7` (2026-03-23) removed someguy with the message:
> "Someguy v0.11.1's IPNS endpoints are non-functional — PUT/GET hang indefinitely, causing every delegated routing publish to timeout. Revert to delegated-ipfs.dev until a working someguy release is available."

The removal included:
- Deletion of someguy service from `docker-compose.staging.yml`
- Revert of `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` to `https://delegated-ipfs.dev`
- Removal of someguy metrics scraping from Alloy config

### **Phase 19 Planning Context**

Phase 19-01 was the planned work to deploy someguy. The plan documents (in `.planning/phases/19-ipns-resolution-improvement/`) specify:
- Image version: `v0.11.1`
- Port: `8190`
- DHT mode: `standard` for staging (to avoid overwhelming resource usage)
- No `depends_on` from API service (API handles routing failures gracefully with DB fallback)
- No host port exposure (internal Docker network only)

### **Key Implementation Notes**

1. **Listen Address**: Must be `0.0.0.0:8190`, not `127.0.0.1:8190`, for Docker inter-container communication
2. **DHT Mode**: `standard` (not `accelerated`) to avoid full DHT crawl and excessive resource consumption
3. **Health Check**: Uses `/version` endpoint, not `/health`
4. **No API Dependency**: The API service does NOT depend on Someguy; if Someguy is slow/unavailable, the API falls back to DB lookups
5. **Metrics Path**: `/debug/metrics/prometheus` for Prometheus scraping

Here is the full report.

---

## Someguy IPNS Research Report

### Repository
`ipfs/someguy` on GitHub -- "A Delegated Routing V1 server and proxy for all your routing needs."

### Latest Version
**v0.11.1** (released 2026-02-16). This is a dependency-bump release updating go-libp2p to v0.47.0, go-libp2p-kad-dht to v0.38.0, and boxo to v0.37.0. No IPNS-specific fixes in this release.

---

### Known IPNS Bugs and Limitations

**1. IPNS only works via Amino DHT -- no PubSub support (open issue #61)**
Someguy resolves IPNS records exclusively via the Amino DHT. IPNS over PubSub is tracked as an open feature request (#61) but has never been implemented. PubSub is expensive and needs topic GC before it can be enabled. The workaround mentioned in #102 is to configure `SOMEGUY_IPNS_ENDPOINTS` to point at a Kubo node that has PubSub enabled.

**2. DHT warm-up / FullRT readiness causes IPNS failures on fresh starts**
Someguy runs a "bundled DHT" with two layers: a FullRT (accelerated) client and a standard DHT client. The `getDHT()` method checks `fullRT.Ready()` and falls back to the standard DHT if not ready. In `accelerated` mode (the default), the FullRT needs time to crawl the routing table. In `standard` mode, the standard DHT is used directly but still needs to bootstrap and populate its routing table. On a fresh container with limited resources (like the CipherBox sidecar with 0.5 CPU / 768MB RAM), this warm-up can be very slow or incomplete, during which time IPNS PUT/GET operations will either hang until the 30-second boxo routing timeout or fail outright.

**3. Resource Manager limits degrade routing (closed issue #64)**
When run in Docker with default resource limits, the accelerated DHT client logs: "Accelerated DHT client was unable to fully refresh its routing table due to Resource Manager limits, which may degrade content routing." This was seen even on the official Docker image and is caused by insufficient `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY` / `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_FD` settings.

**4. Intermittent 404s for routable peers (closed issue #99)**
Someguy sometimes returns 404 for clearly routable peers when go-libp2p-kad-dht hits connection limits and fails DHT lookups with context cancellation. A potential fix exists in go-libp2p-kad-dht v0.29.1 (#1017). This affects IPNS resolution indirectly since IPNS also goes through DHT lookups.

**5. No explicit IPNS-specific timeout in someguy**
The boxo HTTP routing server applies a `DefaultRoutingTimeout = 30 seconds` to all operations including IPNS GET/PUT. Someguy itself does not add any additional timeout. If the DHT is not ready or cannot find the record, the request hangs for the full 30 seconds before timing out.

**6. Streaming NDJSON can hang at delegated-ipfs.dev (closed issue #84)**
This was not a someguy bug but an Nginx/Cloudflare infrastructure issue. Streaming responses with `application/x-ndjson` could hang when going through the CDN layer. Not directly IPNS-related but relevant for understanding public endpoint reliability.

---

### Configuration Requirements for IPNS to Work

**Critical environment variables:**

| Variable | Default | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `SOMEGUY_DHT` | `accelerated` | DHT mode: `accelerated`, `standard`, or `disabled`. IPNS requires DHT to be enabled. |
| `SOMEGUY_IPNS_ENDPOINTS` | `auto` | Comma-separated Delegated Routing V1 endpoints for IPNS. `auto` uses autoconf to fetch from `conf.ipfs-mainnet.org`. |
| `SOMEGUY_AUTOCONF` | `true` | Enables auto-discovery of endpoints and bootstrap peers from the IPFS mainnet config service. |
| `SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS` | `127.0.0.1:8190` | HTTP listen address. |
| `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_LOW` | `100` | Minimum libp2p connections. |
| `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_HIGH` | `3000` | Maximum libp2p connections. |
| `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY` | (system default) | Memory limit for libp2p resource manager. |

**Does someguy need a running Kubo node?** No. Someguy is a standalone binary that runs its own libp2p DHT client. It does NOT proxy to a Kubo node by default. It bootstraps its own DHT connections using bootstrap peers from autoconf (or hardcoded defaults). However, if you want IPNS over PubSub, you would need to point `SOMEGUY_IPNS_ENDPOINTS` at a Kubo node with PubSub enabled.

**Network requirements:**
- Someguy's libp2p node listens on port 4004 (TCP, UDP/QUIC, WebRTC, WebTransport) for DHT participation
- It needs outbound internet access to reach bootstrap peers and participate in the DHT
- It needs outbound HTTPS access if autoconf is enabled (to fetch `conf.ipfs-mainnet.org/autoconf.json`)
- **The CipherBox sidecar config did NOT expose port 4004** -- this likely severely limited DHT participation since other peers could not connect back

---

### Why CipherBox's Someguy Sidecar Was Broken

Based on the Docker Compose configuration in PR #284 (added someguy) and PR #322 (removed it), the sidecar had these problems:

1. **No ports exposed for libp2p**: The someguy service had no `ports:` mapping, meaning port 4004 was not exposed to the host or other containers. The DHT client could make outbound connections but could not receive inbound connections, severely limiting its ability to participate in the DHT and discover IPNS records.

2. **`SOMEGUY_DHT: standard` mode**: This skips the accelerated FullRT client entirely. The standard DHT client is slower to warm up and less effective for lookups.

3. **Tight resource limits**: 0.5 CPU and 768MB RAM with `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY: 512MB` -- the same configuration that triggers the Resource Manager error in issue #64.

4. **Low connection limits**: `CONNMGR_LOW=50, CONNMGR_HIGH=300` vs. defaults of 100 and 3000. Fewer connections = fewer DHT peers = slower/failed IPNS lookups.

The net effect: IPNS PUT/GET requests hung for 30 seconds (the boxo routing timeout) and then failed, because the DHT could never effectively participate in the network.

---

### Fixes and Workarounds

1. **Use `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` instead** (current CipherBox approach after PR #322). This is the public Delegated Routing endpoint operated by IP Shipyard with a proper fleet of someguy instances behind Cloudflare.

2. **If self-hosting someguy, ensure proper configuration:**
   - Expose port 4004 (TCP + UDP) for libp2p
   - Use `SOMEGUY_DHT=accelerated` (the default) for faster routing table population
   - Give sufficient resources: at least 2GB RAM, 1+ CPU
   - Use default connection manager limits (100 low / 3000 high)
   - Set `SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY` high enough (1GB+)
   - Allow a warm-up period of several minutes before expecting reliable IPNS results
   - Consider setting `SOMEGUY_IPNS_ENDPOINTS=https://delegated-ipfs.dev` as a delegated fallback so even before the local DHT is ready, IPNS queries can be proxied

3. **For IPNS over PubSub**: Not supported in someguy. Use a Kubo node with `Ipns.UsePubsub=true` and point `SOMEGUY_IPNS_ENDPOINTS` at its routing API, or use Kubo directly.

> AGENT

Pushed to `origin/fix/someguy-ipns-routing`. You can pull it on the Docker host now.

**Key config changes vs. the failed staging deployment:**

| Setting | Previous (broken) | New |
|---|---|---|
| libp2p port 4004 | Not exposed | Exposed (TCP + UDP) |
| DHT mode | `standard` | `accelerated` |
| Connection limits | 50/300 | 100/3000 (defaults) |
| libp2p memory | 512MB | 1GB |
| Container memory | 768MB | 2GB |
| Container CPU | 0.5 | 1.0 |
| Start period | 30s | 60s |

**After you start it**, give it a couple of minutes to warm up the DHT routing table, then test with:

```bash
# Check it's running
curl http://192.168.133.114:8190/version

# Test IPNS resolve (use any known IPNS key)
curl --max-time 30 http://192.168.133.114:8190/routing/v1/ipns/<key>

# Test providers endpoint (should work immediately)
curl --max-time 30 http://192.168.133.114:8190/routing/v1/providers/<cid>
```

```
     1→import { Injectable, ExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/common';
     2→import { ThrottlerGuard } from '@nestjs/throttler';
     3→import { timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto';
     4→
     5→/**
     6→ * Extends ThrottlerGuard with a bypass header for test environments.
     7→ *
     8→ * When THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set, NODE_ENV is not 'production', and
     9→ * a request includes a matching X-Throttle-Bypass header, rate limiting
    10→ * is skipped. This allows SDK E2E and load tests to run against staging
    11→ * without hitting 429s.
    12→ *
    13→ * Security:
    14→ *   - Production always enforces rate limits (NODE_ENV check)
    15→ *   - If the env var is unset/empty, the header is ignored entirely
    16→ *   - Uses crypto.timingSafeEqual to prevent timing attacks
    17→ */
    18→@Injectable()
    19→export class BypassableThrottlerGuard extends ThrottlerGuard {
    20→  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {
    21→    const secret = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET;
    22→
    23→    if (secret && process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production') {
    24→      const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    25→      const rawHeader = request.headers['x-throttle-bypass'];
    26→      const header = Array.isArray(rawHeader) ? rawHeader[0] : rawHeader;
    27→      if (header && safeEqual(header, secret)) {
    28→        return true;
    29→      }
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    return super.canActivate(context);
    33→  }
    34→}
    35→
    36→/** Constant-time string comparison using Node.js crypto. */
    37→function safeEqual(a: string, b: string): boolean {
    38→  const bufA = Buffer.from(a);
    39→  const bufB = Buffer.from(b);
    40→  if (bufA.length !== bufB.length) return false;
    41→  return timingSafeEqual(bufA, bufB);
    42→}
    43→
```

     1→/**
     2→ * SDK E2E Test Harness
     3→ *
     4→ * Creates authenticated CipherBoxClient instances backed by real API accounts.
     5→ * Extracted from packages/sdk/src/__tests__/integration.test.ts pattern.
     6→ *
     7→ * Each call to createTestContext() creates a unique user via /auth/test-login,
     8→ * initializes their vault, and returns a ready-to-use CipherBoxClient.
     9→ */
    10→
    11→import { CipherBoxClient } from '@cipherbox/sdk';
    12→import { initializeVault, encryptVaultKeys } from '@cipherbox/core';
    13→import { deriveIpnsName, hexToBytes, bytesToHex } from '@cipherbox/crypto';
    14→
    15→const API_URL = process.env.SDK_E2E_API_URL ?? 'http://localhost:3000';
    16→const SECRET = process.env.SDK_E2E_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';
    17→const THROTTLE_BYPASS = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET ?? '';
    18→
    19→/**
    20→ * Build default headers for fetch calls.
    21→ * Includes the throttle bypass header when THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set.
    22→ */
    23→function fetchHeaders(extra: Record<string, string> = {}): Record<string, string> {
    24→  const headers: Record<string, string> = { ...extra };
    25→  if (THROTTLE_BYPASS) {
    26→    headers['X-Throttle-Bypass'] = THROTTLE_BYPASS;
    27→  }
    28→  return headers;
    29→}
    30→
    31→/** Default headers for the api-client axios instance (throttle bypass). */
    32→function axiosDefaultHeaders(): Record<string, string> | undefined {
    33→  return THROTTLE_BYPASS ? { 'X-Throttle-Bypass': THROTTLE_BYPASS } : undefined;
    34→}
    35→
    36→/** Core account data returned by createTestAccount (shared between sdk-e2e and load tests). */
    37→export interface TestAccount {
    38→  client: CipherBoxClient;
    39→  accessToken: string;
    40→  publicKey: Uint8Array;
    41→  privateKey: Uint8Array;
    42→  rootIpnsName: string;
    43→  rootFolderKey: Uint8Array;
    44→  rootIpnsKeypair: { publicKey: Uint8Array; privateKey: Uint8Array };
    45→  email: string;
    46→}
    47→
    48→export interface TestContext extends TestAccount {
    49→  /** Destroy the client and zero key material */
    50→  cleanup: () => void;
    51→}
    52→
    53→export interface CreateAccountOptions {
    54→  apiUrl?: string;
    55→  secret?: string;
    56→  label: string;
    57→  emailPrefix?: string;
    58→}
    59→
    60→/**
    61→ * Create a test account with an initialized vault and CipherBoxClient.
    62→ * This is the shared core used by both sdk-e2e (createTestContext) and
    63→ * load tests (createPoolClient).
    64→ */
    65→export async function createTestAccount(opts: CreateAccountOptions): Promise<TestAccount> {
    66→  const apiUrl = opts.apiUrl ?? API_URL;
    67→  const secret = opts.secret ?? SECRET;
    68→  const prefix = opts.emailPrefix ?? 'sdk-e2e';
    69→  const email = `${prefix}-${opts.label}-${Date.now()}@example.com`;
    70→
    71→  // 1. Authenticate
    72→  const loginRes = await fetch(`${apiUrl}/auth/test-login`, {
    73→    method: 'POST',
    74→    headers: fetchHeaders({ 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }),
    75→    body: JSON.stringify({ email, secret }),
    76→  });
    77→  if (!loginRes.ok) {
    78→    throw new Error(`test-login failed (${loginRes.status}): ${await loginRes.text()}`);
    79→  }
    80→  const { accessToken, publicKeyHex, privateKeyHex } = await loginRes.json();
    81→  const publicKey = hexToBytes(publicKeyHex);
    82→  const privateKey = hexToBytes(privateKeyHex);
    83→
    84→  // Steps 2-4 wrapped in try/catch to clean up the test account on failure
    85→  try {
    86→    // 2. Initialize vault
    87→    const vault = await initializeVault(privateKey);
    88→    const encrypted = await encryptVaultKeys(vault, publicKey);
    89→    const rootIpnsName = await deriveIpnsName(vault.rootIpnsKeypair.publicKey);
    90→
    91→    // 3. Register vault on server
    92→    const initRes = await fetch(`${apiUrl}/vault/init`, {
    93→      method: 'POST',
    94→      headers: fetchHeaders({
    95→        Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}`,
    96→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
    97→      }),
    98→      body: JSON.stringify({
    99→        ownerPublicKey: bytesToHex(publicKey),
   100→        encryptedRootFolderKey: bytesToHex(encrypted.encryptedRootFolderKey),
   101→        encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey: bytesToHex(encrypted.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey),
   102→        rootIpnsName,
   103→      }),
   104→    });
   105→    if (!initRes.ok) {
   106→      throw new Error(`vault/init failed (${initRes.status}): ${await initRes.text()}`);
   107→    }
   108→
   109→    // 4. Create CipherBoxClient with instance-scoped axios (no singleton needed)
   110→    const client = new CipherBoxClient({
   111→      apiUrl,
   112→      getAccessToken: async () => accessToken,
   113→      vaultKeypair: { publicKey, privateKey },
   114→      rootIpnsName,
   115→      rootFolderKey: vault.rootFolderKey,
   116→      defaultHeaders: axiosDefaultHeaders(),
   117→    });
   118→    client.registerFolder(rootIpnsName, vault.rootFolderKey, vault.rootIpnsKeypair, [], 0n);
   119→
   120→    return {
   121→      client,
   122→      accessToken,
   123→      publicKey,
   124→      privateKey,
   125→      rootIpnsName,
   126→      rootFolderKey: vault.rootFolderKey,
   127→      rootIpnsKeypair: vault.rootIpnsKeypair,
   128→      email,
   129→    };
   130→  } catch (err) {
   131→    // Clean up partially provisioned account to avoid leaks
   132→    await deleteTestAccount({ accessToken }, apiUrl);
   133→    throw err;
   134→  }
   135→}
   136→
   137→/**
   138→ * Create a fully-initialized test context (convenience wrapper over createTestAccount).
   139→ */
   140→export async function createTestContext(label: string): Promise<TestContext> {
   141→  const account = await createTestAccount({ label });
   142→  return { ...account, cleanup: () => account.client.destroy() };
   143→}
   144→
   145→/**
   146→ * Delete a test account (cleanup after tests).
   147→ */
   148→export async function deleteTestAccount(
   149→  ctx: { accessToken: string },
   150→  apiUrl = API_URL
   151→): Promise<void> {
   152→  try {
   153→    const res = await fetch(`${apiUrl}/auth/account`, {
   154→      method: 'DELETE',
   155→      headers: fetchHeaders({
   156→        Authorization: `Bearer ${ctx.accessToken}`,
   157→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   158→      }),
   159→      body: JSON.stringify({ confirmation: 'DELETE' }),
   160→    });
   161→    if (!res.ok) {
   162→      console.warn(`Account deletion failed (${res.status}): ${await res.text()}`);
   163→    }
   164→  } catch (err) {
   165→    console.warn(`Account deletion error: ${(err as Error).message}`);
   166→  }
   167→}
   168→
   169→/**
   170→ * fetch() wrapper that automatically injects the throttle bypass header.
   171→ * Use instead of raw fetch() in test suites to avoid 429s.
   172→ */
   173→export function testFetch(url: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {
   174→  const headers = new Headers(init?.headers);
   175→  if (THROTTLE_BYPASS) {
   176→    headers.set('X-Throttle-Bypass', THROTTLE_BYPASS);
   177→  }
   178→  return fetch(url, { ...init, headers });
   179→}
   180→
   181→export { API_URL, SECRET, fetchHeaders };
   182→

     1→import axios, {
     2→  type AxiosInstance,
     3→  type AxiosRequestConfig,
     4→  type AxiosError,
     5→  type AxiosProgressEvent,
     6→  type CancelToken,
     7→} from 'axios';
     8→
     9→export type ApiClientConfig = {
    10→  /** Base URL for the CipherBox API (e.g., "http://localhost:3000") */
    11→  baseUrl: string;
    12→  /** Returns a valid access token. Consumer owns refresh logic. */
    13→  getAccessToken: () => Promise<string>;
    14→  /** Called on 401 response to obtain a new access token. If provided, the failed request is retried once with the new token. */
    15→  refreshAccessToken?: () => Promise<string>;
    16→  /** Send cookies cross-origin (needed for HTTP-only refresh token cookie in web). */
    17→  withCredentials?: boolean;
    18→  /** Called when token refresh fails (e.g., clear stores, redirect to login). */
    19→  onRefreshFailure?: () => void;
    20→  /** Extra headers sent with every request (e.g., throttle bypass for testing). */
    21→  defaultHeaders?: Record<string, string>;
    22→};
    23→
    24→/**
    25→ * Create a configured axios instance for CipherBox API calls.
    26→ * No Zustand, no import.meta.env -- all config injected by consumer.
    27→ *
    28→ * Includes:
    29→ * - Request interceptor: injects Bearer token from getAccessToken()
    30→ * - Response interceptor (optional): 401 refresh + retry when refreshAccessToken is provided
    31→ */
    32→export function createAxiosInstance(config: ApiClientConfig): AxiosInstance {
    33→  const instance = axios.create({
    34→    baseURL: config.baseUrl,
    35→    withCredentials: config.withCredentials ?? false,
    36→    headers: config.defaultHeaders,
    37→  });
    38→
    39→  // Request interceptor: inject auth token
    40→  instance.interceptors.request.use(async (reqConfig) => {
    41→    const token = await config.getAccessToken();

No libp2p port (4004) exposed — DHT couldn't receive inbound connections\n2. DHT mode set to 'standard' instead of 'accelerated' (default)\n3. Resource limits too tight (0.5 CPU, 768MB RAM, 512MB libp2p memory)\n\nFix: expose port 4004 (TCP+UDP), use accelerated DHT mode, allocate\n2GB RAM / 1 CPU / 1GB libp2p memory, and increase start period to 60s.\n\nVerified locally: someguy IPNS GET resolves in ~0.5s, PUT in ~0.1s,\nand all 83 SDK E2E tests pass with someguy as the routing backend.\n\nAlso restores someguy Prometheus metrics scraping in Alloy config\nand updates deploy-staging workflow to use http://someguy:8190.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",

     3→This folder contains learnings from AI-assisted development sessions. Each entry captures insights that help future sessions work more effectively in this codebase.
     4→
     5→## When to Create an Entry
     6→
     7→Create an entry after completing a significant task that involved:
     8→- Learning something non-obvious about the codebase
     9→- Getting stuck and finding a solution
    10→- Discovering patterns or gotchas worth remembering
    11→
    12→## File Naming
    13→

    15→
    16→Example: `2026-02-06-discord-style-usernames.md`
    17→
    18→## Entry Structure

    21→# [Brief Title]
    22→
    23→**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD
    24→
    25→## Original Prompt
    26→
    27→> [Full prompt text as provided by user - preserving exact wording helps improve future prompts]
    28→
    29→## What I Learned
    30→
    31→Bullet points of non-obvious discoveries:
    32→- Gotchas and edge cases
    33→- Codebase patterns
    34→- Data structure quirks
    35→- Things that look similar but behave differently
    36→
    37→## What Would Have Helped
    38→
    39→Information that would have made the task smoother if known upfront:
    40→- Missing context from the prompt
    41→- Files I should have checked first
    42→- Clarifying questions I should have asked
    43→
    44→## Key Files
    45→
    46→List of files most relevant to this type of task (for future reference).
    47→```
    48→
    49→## Guidelines
    50→
    51→- **Be concise** - Future you wants quick insights, not a novel
    52→- **Focus on learnings** - Skip implementation details that are obvious from the code
    53→- **Highlight gotchas** - The non-obvious stuff is most valuable
    54→- **Think reusability** - What would help with similar tasks in the future?
    55→

id: toolu_01Rtj3C9YW8a8LtzttTtqUjP
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.learnings/2026-03-23-vitest-env-vars-not-auto-loaded.md",
  "content": "# Vitest Does Not Auto-Load .env Into process.env\n\n**Date:** 2026-03-23\n\n## Original Prompt\n\n> Run SDK E2E tests against someguy\n\n## What I Learned\n\n- **Vitest does not load `.env` files into `process.env` by default.** Unlike tools like Jest with `dotenv`, or the NestJS CLI which loads `.env` automatically, vitest uses Vite's env handling which only exposes `VITE_`-prefixed vars to the client. Server-side `process.env` reads in test code will only see vars inherited from the shell.\n- Creating a `.env` file in `tests/sdk-e2e/` had **no effect** on `process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET` inside the test harness. The tests continued hitting 429s because the bypass header was empty.\n- The fix is to pass env vars explicitly when invoking the test command:\n  ```bash\n  REDACTED pnpm --filter sdk-e2e test\n  ```\n- Alternative: add `dotenv/config` to the vitest setup file, or configure `envDir` + `envPrefix: ''` in `vitest.config.ts`.\n- This caused 14 spurious 429 failures that looked like the someguy IPNS fix wasn't working, when in reality the throttle bypass secret just wasn't reaching the test process.\n\n## What Would Have Helped\n\n- Checking the test harness's `process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET` value with a quick `console.log` would have immediately revealed the env var wasn't loaded.\n- The `.env.example` in `tests/sdk-e2e/` implied a `.env` file should work, but vitest doesn't honour it without explicit config.\n\n## Key Files\n\n- `tests/sdk-e2e/vitest.config.ts` — no dotenv/envDir config\n- `tests/sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness.ts:17` — reads `THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET` from `process.env`\n- `apps/api/src/common/guards/throttler-bypass.guard.ts` — the bypass guard that expects the header\n"
}
```

     6→ */
     7→
     8→import { describe, it, afterAll } from 'vitest';
     9→import {
    10→  createClientPool,
    11→  destroyClientPool,
    12→  aggregateAndReport,
    13→  type PoolClient,
    14→} from '../harness/client-pool';
    15→import { runFolderWorkload } from '../workloads/folder-workload';
    16→
    17→const NUM_CLIENTS = parseInt(process.env.LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS ?? '20', 10);
    18→const CYCLES_PER_CLIENT = 50;
    19→
    20→describe('IPNS Publish Storm', () => {
    21→  let pool: PoolClient[] = [];
    22→
    23→  afterAll(async () => {
    24→    await destroyClientPool(pool);
    25→  });
    26→
    27→  it(`${NUM_CLIENTS} clients × ${CYCLES_PER_CLIENT} folder create-rename-delete cycles`, async () => {
    28→    pool = await createClientPool({
    29→      clientCount: NUM_CLIENTS,
    30→      label: 'ipns-storm',
    31→    });
    32→
    33→    const results = await Promise.allSettled(
    34→      pool.map((pc) => {
    35→        pc.metrics.start();
    36→        return runFolderWorkload(pc, { cycles: CYCLES_PER_CLIENT }).finally(() =>
    37→          pc.metrics.stop()
    38→        );
    39→      })
    40→    );
    41→
    42→    const succeeded = results.filter((r) => r.status === 'fulfilled').length;
    43→    console.log(`\nClients completed: ${succeeded}/${pool.length}`);
    44→
    45→    await aggregateAndReport('IPNS Publish Storm', pool);
    46→  });
    47→});
    48→

```
     1→/**
     2→ * Load Test Client Pool
     3→ *
     4→ * Manages N CipherBoxClient instances, each with its own test account.
     5→ * Creates accounts in parallel, distributes workloads, and collects metrics.
     6→ *
     7→ * Reuses the shared createTestAccount/deleteTestAccount from sdk-e2e
     8→ * to avoid duplicating the 5-step account provisioning sequence.
     9→ */
    10→
    11→import {
    12→  createTestAccount,
    13→  deleteTestAccount,
    14→  type TestAccount,
    15→} from '../../../sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness';
    16→import { MetricsCollector, type OperationMetrics } from './metrics';
    17→import { printSummary, toJsonReport } from './reporter';
    18→
    19→const API_URL = process.env.LOAD_TEST_API_URL ?? 'http://localhost:3000';
    20→const SECRET = process.env.LOAD_TEST_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';
    21→
    22→export interface PoolClient extends TestAccount {
    23→  id: number;
    24→  metrics: MetricsCollector;
    25→}
    26→
    27→export interface ClientPoolOptions {
    28→  clientCount: number;
    29→  label: string;
    30→}
    31→
    32→/**
    33→ * Create a pool of N authenticated CipherBoxClient instances.
    34→ *
    35→ * Each client has its own test account, vault, metrics collector, and
    36→ * instance-scoped axios (no shared singleton).
    37→ */
    38→export async function createClientPool(opts: ClientPoolOptions): Promise<PoolClient[]> {
    39→  const { clientCount, label } = opts;
    40→  console.log(`Creating ${clientCount} test accounts for "${label}"...`);
    41→  const start = performance.now();
    42→
    43→  // Create accounts in parallel batches of 5 to avoid overwhelming the API
    44→  const clients: PoolClient[] = [];
    45→  const batchSize = 5;
    46→
    47→  for (let batch = 0; batch < clientCount; batch += batchSize) {
    48→    const batchEnd = Math.min(batch + batchSize, clientCount);
    49→    const promises = [];
    50→
    51→    for (let i = batch; i < batchEnd; i++) {
    52→      promises.push(
    53→        createTestAccount({
    54→          apiUrl: API_URL,
    55→          secret: SECRET,
    56→          label: `${label}-${i}`,
    57→          emailPrefix: 'load',
    58→        }).then((account): PoolClient => ({ ...account, id: i, metrics: new MetricsCollector() }))
    59→      );
    60→    }
    61→
    62→    const results = await Promise.allSettled(promises);
    63→    for (const result of results) {
    64→      if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {
    65→        clients.push(result.value);
    66→      } else {
    67→        console.warn(`Failed to create pool client: ${result.reason}`);
    68→      }
    69→    }
    70→  }
    71→
    72→  const elapsed = performance.now() - start;
    73→  console.log(
    74→    `Created ${clients.length}/${clientCount} clients in ${(elapsed / 1000).toFixed(1)}s`
    75→  );
    76→
    77→  return clients;
    78→}
    79→
    80→/**
    81→ * Destroy all clients in the pool and delete test accounts.
    82→ */
    83→export async function destroyClientPool(pool: PoolClient[]): Promise<void> {
    84→  console.log(`Cleaning up ${pool.length} test accounts...`);
    85→  for (const pc of pool) {
    86→    pc.client.destroy();
    87→    // Zero harness-owned key material (client.destroy() only clears its internal copies)
    88→    pc.privateKey.fill(0);
    89→    pc.rootFolderKey.fill(0);
    90→    pc.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey.fill(0);
    91→    await deleteTestAccount(pc, API_URL);
    92→  }
    93→}
    94→
    95→/**
    96→ * Aggregate metrics from all pool clients, print summary, and return metrics.
    97→ * Extracts the repeated boilerplate from every load test scenario.
    98→ */
    99→export async function aggregateAndReport(
   100→  scenarioName: string,
   101→  pool: PoolClient[]
   102→): Promise<OperationMetrics[]> {
   103→  const allMetrics = new MetricsCollector();
   104→
   105→  let minTimestamp = Infinity;
   106→  let maxTimestamp = 0;
   107→
   108→  for (const pc of pool) {
   109→    for (const sample of pc.metrics.getReadonlySamples()) {
   110→      allMetrics.record(sample);
   111→      if (sample.timestamp < minTimestamp) minTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
   112→      if (sample.timestamp > maxTimestamp) maxTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
   113→    }
   114→  }
   115→
   116→  // Set collector timing from sample timestamps for accurate throughput calculation
   117→  allMetrics.setElapsedMs(maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 1);
   118→
   119→  const totalDuration = maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 0;
   120→  const metrics = allMetrics.getMetrics();
   121→
   122→  printSummary(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
   123→
   124→  // Write JSON report to file for CI artifact upload
   125→  const reportJson = toJsonReport(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
   126→  console.log('\nJSON Report:\n' + reportJson);
   127→
   128→  try {
   129→    const fs = await import('node:fs/promises');
   130→    const slug = scenarioName.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-');
   131→    await fs.writeFile(`metrics-${slug}.json`, reportJson);
   132→  } catch (err) {
   133→    console.warn(`Failed to write metrics report: ${(err as Error).message}`);
   134→  }
   135→
   136→  return metrics;
   137→}
   138→
```

> AGENT

Interesting results. Here's the comparison:

**IPNS Publish Storm — 5 clients x 50 cycles (750 ops)**

| Metric | Someguy (self-hosted) | delegated-ipfs.dev | Delta |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Duration** | 89.3s | 69.7s | +28% slower |
| **Throughput** | 8.39 ops/s | 10.75 ops/s | -22% |
| **Errors** | 0 | 0 | same |
| **createFolder p50** | 727ms | 606ms | +20% |
| **createFolder p95** | 1.07s | 1.22s | -12% (someguy better) |
| **createFolder p99** | 1.27s | 1.62s | -22% (someguy better) |
| **deleteItem p50** | 469ms | 395ms | +19% |
| **renameItem p50** | 520ms | 305ms | +70% |
| **renameItem p95** | 1.06s | 704ms | +51% |

Someguy has **higher median latency** but **lower tail latency** (p95/p99) for creates. delegated-ipfs.dev is faster at median but has higher variance. Both have zero errors at this load level.

The median difference makes sense — someguy is publishing to the DHT on our single-node sidecar, while delegated-ipfs.dev has a large fleet. But the tighter p99 means someguy is more predictable.

> AGENT

Here's the full comparison:

---

## Load Test Results: Someguy vs delegated-ipfs.dev

### IPNS Publish Storm (5 clients x 50 cycles = 750 ops)

| Metric | Someguy | delegated-ipfs.dev | Winner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Duration | 89.3s | 69.7s | delegated |
| Throughput | 8.39 ops/s | 10.75 ops/s | delegated |
| Errors | **0** | **0** | tie |
| createFolder p50 | 728ms | 606ms | delegated |
| createFolder p99 | **1.27s** | 1.62s | **someguy** |
| deleteItem p50 | 469ms | 395ms | delegated |
| renameItem p50 | 520ms | 305ms | delegated |
| renameItem p95 | 1.06s | 704ms | delegated |

### Mixed Workload (5 clients x 45 mixed ops)

| Metric | Someguy | delegated-ipfs.dev | Winner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Duration | 58.9s | 40.4s | delegated |
| Throughput | 3.68 ops/s | 5.50 ops/s | delegated |
| Errors | **0** | **0** | tie |
| createFolder p50 | 1.4s | 840ms | delegated |
| createFolder p99 | **2.2s** | 2.4s | **someguy** |
| uploadFile p50 | 1.5s | 1.1s | delegated |
| uploadFile p99 | **2.4s** | 2.7s | **someguy** |

### Summary

- **delegated-ipfs.dev wins on median latency and throughput** — their fleet is much larger than a single-node sidecar
- **Someguy wins on tail latency (p99)** — more predictable, no CDN/Cloudflare variability
- **Both have zero errors** — the critical fix is that someguy now actually works (it was hanging before)
- The key value of self-hosted someguy is **reliability and independence** — delegated-ipfs.dev has had outages and 502s in the past

The median latency gap would shrink on staging with a warmer DHT routing table (our sidecar was freshly started). The p99 advantage matters more for user experience.

Should I commit the learnings file and push, or would you like to adjust anything first?

> DEVELOPER

ok so in your opinion it makes sense to use the someguy sidecar?

> AGENT

Yes, I think it does. Here's my reasoning:

**The core argument is reliability, not speed.** delegated-ipfs.dev is faster at median because it's backed by a fleet, but it's a third-party service you don't control. The todo itself was created because it returned 502s and was unreliable — that's what motivated Phase 19 in the first place. A 40% p50 latency hit is acceptable when the alternative is an external dependency that can go down with no recourse.

**The p99 advantage matters more than the p50 disadvantage.** Users feel tail latency. A consistent 700ms is better UX than usually-300ms-but-sometimes-2.4s. Someguy's tighter distribution means fewer "why is this taking forever" moments.

**The median gap will shrink over time.** Our test was against a freshly started sidecar with a cold DHT. On staging, after hours/days of operation, the routing table fills up and lookups get faster. The fleet advantage of delegated-ipfs.dev matters less as the sidecar warms.

**Two caveats worth considering:**

1. **Resource cost** — someguy needs 2GB RAM + 1 CPU on the staging VPS. If that's tight, it matters.
2. **Fallback option** — the `DelegatedRoutingClient` already has retry logic with timeouts. You could configure `SOMEGUY_IPNS_ENDPOINTS=https://delegated-ipfs.dev` on the someguy container itself so it proxies to the public fleet when its local DHT doesn't have a record. Best of both worlds — local-first with a remote fallback — but adds complexity.

For a technology demonstrator where the goal is proving the architecture works end-to-end with self-hosted infra, someguy is the right call. Would you like me to commit what we have and open a PR?

> DEVELOPER

ok I am running it on a hostinger kvm2 vps, so it should handle the load since the rest of the stack should not be loading the system too much.

> AGENT

Good, KVM2 should be plenty. Let me commit the learnings file, push, and open the PR.

## Research Summary: Prometheus Metrics Setup in CipherBox

I've thoroughly researched the Prometheus metrics infrastructure in the CipherBox codebase. Here's what I found:

### 1. Metrics Library Used

**Library:** `prom-client` v15.1.3 (in `apps/api/package.json` line 47)

This is the canonical Node.js Prometheus client library. No NestJS-specific wrapper (`@willsoto/nestjs-prometheus`) is used.

---

### 2. Metrics Architecture

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/metrics/`

The metrics system consists of:

- **MetricsService** (`metrics.service.ts`) — Central singleton managing all metrics
  - Custom `Registry` instance (`prom-client.Registry`)
  - Exposes gauges, counters, and histograms
  - Runs async gauge collection every 30 seconds via `onModuleInit`

- **MetricsController** (`metrics.controller.ts`) — Exposes `/metrics` endpoint
  - Serves Prometheus text format at `GET /metrics`
  - Excluded from HTTP metrics interceptor to avoid self-referential noise

- **HttpMetricsInterceptor** (`http-metrics.interceptor.ts`) — Global request tracker
  - Registered as `APP_INTERCEPTOR` in `app.module.ts` line 114-116
  - Records `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds` histogram for all HTTP requests
  - Normalizes route paths (replaces UUIDs and CIDs with placeholders for low cardinality)
  - Ignores `/metrics` endpoint

- **MetricsModule** (`metrics.module.ts`) — Global module
  - Marked `@Global()` for dependency injection across the app
  - Exports `MetricsService` for injection everywhere

---

### 3. How to Register New Metrics

In `MetricsService` constructor (lines 56–191):

**For Counters:**
```typescript
readonly myCounter = new client.Counter({
  name: 'cipherbox_my_operation_total',
  help: 'Description of what is counted',
  labelNames: ['label1', 'label2'],  // Optional labels
  registers: [this.registry],
});
```

**For Gauges (DB-polled state):**
```typescript
readonly myGauge = new client.Gauge({
  name: 'cipherbox_my_state',
  help: 'Description of gauge',
  labelNames: ['label1'],
  registers: [this.registry],
});
```

**For Histograms (latency/duration):**
```typescript
readonly myHistogram = new client.Histogram({
  name: 'cipherbox_my_operation_seconds',
  help: 'Description of operation duration',
  labelNames: ['status', 'outcome'],
  buckets: [0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 5, 10],  // Custom bucket boundaries
  registers: [this.registry],
});
```

---

### 4. Pattern for Adding Metrics to a Service

**For Counters (increment on events):**
```typescript
constructor(private readonly metricsService: MetricsService) {}

async someMethod() {
  this.metricsService.myCounter.inc({ label1: 'value1' });
}
```

**For Histograms (measure duration):**

Using `startTimer()` + end callback (recommended):
```typescript
async resolveRecord(ipnsName: string) {
  const endTimer = this.metricsService.ipfsIpnsDuration.startTimer({
    operation: 'resolve',
    source: 'network',
  });
  
  try {
    // Do work
    return result;
  } catch (error) {
    throw error;
  } finally {
    endTimer({ result: 'success', source: 'network' });
  }
}
```

Or manual `.observe()`:
```typescript
const startTime = process.hrtime.bigint();
// Do work
const durationSec = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - startTime) / 1e9;
this.metricsService.myHistogram.observe({ status: 'ok' }, durationSec);
```

---

### 5. Existing IPNS-Related Metrics

**Counters:**
- `cipherbox_ipns_publishes_total` (lines 117–122)
  - Label: `type`
  - Used in: `ipns.service.ts` (publish operations)

- `cipherbox_ipns_resolves_total` (lines 124–129)
  - Label: `source`
  - Defined but not currently incremented in code

**Histograms:**
- `cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds` (lines 160–166) — **Main histogram**
  - Labels: `operation` (publish/resolve), `result` (success/error), `source` (network/db_cache)
  - Buckets: `[0.001, 0.005, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10, 20, 30]` seconds
  - Used in: `ipns.service.ts` (lines 48, 301) for both publish and resolve operations

- `cipherbox_ipns_publish_duration_seconds` (lines 184–190)
  - Label: `outcome` (success/error)
  - Buckets: `[0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 60]` seconds
  - Used in: `ipns.service.ts` (line 91) for delegated routing publish timing

- `cipherbox_ipns_resolve_duration_seconds` (lines 176–182)
  - Labels: `source` (network/db_cache), `outcome` (success/error)
  - Buckets: `[0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30]` seconds
  - Used in: `ipns.service.ts` (line 384) for end-to-end resolve timing

**Gauges:**
- `cipherbox_ipns_entries_total` (lines 78–83)
  - Label: `record_type`
  - DB-polled every 30s from `FolderIpns` table

---

### 6. Metrics Scraping Configuration

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/docker/alloy-config.river` (lines 59–67)

- Scrapes CipherBox API at `http://api:3000/metrics` every 30 seconds
- Forwards to Grafana Cloud Mimir (Prometheus-compatible backend)
- Also scrapes Kubo IPFS metrics (`/debug/metrics/prometheus`) and Someguy delegated router metrics

---

### 7. Default Labels & Naming Convention

**Global default label:**
```typescript
this.registry.setDefaultLabels({ app: 'cipherbox-api' });
```

All metrics are prefixed with `cipherbox_` and follow Prometheus conventions:
- Counters end with `_total`
- Gauges have no suffix
- Histograms end with `_seconds`
- Gauge buckets: no suffix (just `_bucket` in Prometheus output)

---

### 8. Key Implementation Details

- **Timer pattern:** Use `startTimer({ ...initialLabels })` → `endTimer({ ...finalLabels })` for clean async error handling
- **Gauge collection:** Happens asynchronously in the background; if DB isn't ready, logs a warning and continues
- **Cardinality:** Route normalization in the HTTP interceptor keeps cardinality low by replacing dynamic IDs with placeholders
- **Label reset:** For gauges with dynamic labels, `reset()` is called before setting new values to avoid stale data

---

**Summary files referenced:**
- Metrics definition: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/metrics/metrics.service.ts`
- Usage example (IPNS): `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts`
- Usage example (Auth): `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.controller.ts`
- Scraping config: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/docker/alloy-config.river`

Good question — no, it wouldn't. Someguy's `SOMEGUY_IPNS_ENDPOINTS` does concurrent fanout (queries its DHT AND the delegated endpoint in parallel, returns whichever wins). It's not a fallback — it's a race — and someguy's Prometheus metrics don't expose which source won a given request.

So the better design is: **skip the someguy-level config entirely** and implement the fallback at the API level in `DelegatedRoutingClient`, where we control the metrics. That way:

- Primary request goes to someguy
- If someguy fails after retries, fallback to delegated-ipfs.dev
- Prometheus counters track `{backend="primary"}` vs `{backend="fallback"}` for every operation

This gives full visibility. Let me implement it.

   193→  async onModuleInit(): Promise<void> {
   194→    // Attempt an immediate collection; log and continue if DB isn't ready yet
   195→    await this.collectGauges().catch((err: unknown) => {
   196→      this.logger.warn(
   197→        `Initial gauge collection failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`
   198→      );
   199→    });
   200→    this.collectInterval = setInterval(() => {
   201→      this.collectGauges().catch((err: unknown) => {
   202→        this.logger.warn(
   203→          `Gauge collection failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`
   204→        );
   205→      });
   206→    }, 30_000);
   207→    this.logger.log('Prometheus metrics initialized (collecting gauges every 30s)');
   208→  }
   209→
   210→  onModuleDestroy(): void {
   211→    if (this.collectInterval) {
   212→      clearInterval(this.collectInterval);
   213→    }
   214→  }
   215→
   216→  async getMetrics(): Promise<string> {
   217→    return this.registry.metrics();
   218→  }
   219→
   220→  getContentType(): string {
   221→    return this.registry.contentType;
   222→  }
   223→
   224→  private async collectGauges(): Promise<void> {
   225→    const [userCount, fileStats, ipnsByType, republishByStatus] = await Promise.all([
   226→      this.userRepository.count(),
   227→      this.pinnedCidRepository
   228→        .createQueryBuilder('pin')
   229→        .select('COUNT(*)', 'count')
   230→        .addSelect('COALESCE(SUM(pin.size_bytes), 0)', 'totalBytes')
   231→        .getRawOne<{ count: string; totalBytes: string }>(),
   232→      this.folderIpnsRepository
   233→        .createQueryBuilder('ipns')
   234→        .select('ipns.record_type', 'recordType')
   235→        .addSelect('COUNT(*)', 'count')
   236→        .groupBy('ipns.record_type')
   237→        .getRawMany<{ recordType: string; count: string }>(),
   238→      this.republishScheduleRepository
   239→        .createQueryBuilder('sched')
   240→        .select('sched.status', 'status')
   241→        .addSelect('COUNT(*)', 'count')
   242→        .groupBy('sched.status')
   243→        .getRawMany<{ status: string; count: string }>(),
   244→    ]);
   245→
   246→    this.usersTotal.set(userCount);
   247→    this.filesTotal.set(parseInt(fileStats?.count ?? '0', 10));
   248→    this.storageBytesTotal.set(parseInt(fileStats?.totalBytes ?? '0', 10));
   249→
   250→    // Reset IPNS gauges before setting to avoid stale labels
   251→    this.ipnsEntriesTotal.reset();
   252→    for (const row of ipnsByType) {
   253→      this.ipnsEntriesTotal.labels(row.recordType).set(parseInt(row.count, 10));
   254→    }
   255→
   256→    // Reset republish gauges before setting
   257→    this.republishScheduleTotal.reset();
   258→    for (const row of republishByStatus) {
   259→      this.republishScheduleTotal.labels(row.status).set(parseInt(row.count, 10));
   260→    }
   261→  }
   262→}
   263→

     2→import { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';

     4→/** Typed error for non-retryable HTTP responses from the delegated routing API. */
     5→class DelegatedRoutingHttpError extends Error {
     6→  constructor(public readonly status: number) {
     7→    super(`Delegated routing returned ${status}`);

    12→@Injectable()
    13→export class DelegatedRoutingClient {
    14→  private readonly logger = new Logger(DelegatedRoutingClient.name);
    15→  private readonly delegatedRoutingUrl: string;
    16→  private readonly maxRetries = 3;
    17→  private readonly baseDelayMs = 1000;
    18→  private readonly maxRetryDelayMs = 30_000;
    19→  private readonly requestTimeoutMs = 10_000;
    20→
    21→  constructor(private readonly configService: ConfigService) {
    22→    this.delegatedRoutingUrl = this.configService.get<string>(
    23→      'DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL',
    24→      'https://delegated-ipfs.dev'
    25→    );
    26→  }
    27→
    28→  /**
    29→   * Publish an IPNS record via the delegated routing PUT API with exponential backoff retry.
    30→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on persistent failures.
    31→   */
    32→  async publish(ipnsName: string, recordBytes: Uint8Array): Promise<void> {
    33→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`;
    34→
    35→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
    36→
    37→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
    38→      try {
    39→        const { ok, status, retryAfter, errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(
    40→          url,
    41→          {
    42→            method: 'PUT',
    43→            headers: {
    44→              'Content-Type': 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
    45→            },
    46→            body: recordBytes as unknown as BodyInit,
    47→          },
    48→          async (response) => ({
    49→            ok: response.ok,
    50→            status: response.status,
    51→            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),
    52→            errorText: !response.ok && response.status !== 429 ? await response.text() : '',
    53→          })
    54→        );
    55→
    56→        if (ok) {
    57→          this.logger.log(`IPNS record published successfully for ${ipnsName}`);
    58→          return;
    59→        }
    60→
    61→        // Handle rate limiting
    62→        if (status === 429) {
    63→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
    64→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
    65→
    66→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS publish, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
    67→            await this.delay(delayMs);
    68→          }
    69→          continue;
    70→        }
    71→
    72→        // Non-retryable HTTP error
    73→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
    74→        this.logger.error(`Delegated routing returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`);
    75→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);

    79→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
    80→          throw new HttpException(

    82→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
    83→          );
    84→        }
    85→
    86→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));

    88→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
    89→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
    90→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);

    92→            `IPNS publish attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
    93→          );

    95→        }
    96→      }
    97→    }
    98→

   100→    this.logger.error(

   102→    );
   103→    throw new HttpException(

   105→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   106→    );
   107→  }
   108→
   109→  /**
   110→   * Resolve an IPNS name via the delegated routing GET API with retries.
   111→   * Returns the raw record bytes, or null if the IPNS name is not found (404).
   112→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on routing failures.
   113→   */
   114→  async resolve(ipnsName: string): Promise<Uint8Array | null> {
   115→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`;
   116→
   117→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
   118→
   119→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
   120→      try {
   121→        const { ok, status, retryAfter, data, errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(
   122→          url,
   123→          {
   124→            method: 'GET',
   125→            headers: {
   126→              Accept: 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
   127→            },
   128→          },
   129→          async (response) => ({
   130→            ok: response.ok,
   131→            status: response.status,
   132→            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),
   133→            data: response.ok ? new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer()) : null,
   134→            errorText:
   135→              !response.ok && response.status !== 404 && response.status !== 429
   136→                ? await response.text()
   137→                : '',
   138→          })
   139→        );
   140→
   141→        // 404 means IPNS name not found - not an error
   142→        if (status === 404) {
   143→          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name not found: ${ipnsName}`);
   144→          return null;
   145→        }
   146→
   147→        if (ok) {
   148→          return data;
   149→        }
   150→
   151→        // Handle rate limiting
   152→        if (status === 429) {
   153→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   154→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
   155→
   156→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS resolve, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
   157→            await this.delay(delayMs);
   158→          }
   159→          continue;
   160→        }
   161→
   162→        // Non-retryable error
   163→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
   164→        this.logger.error(
   165→          `Delegated routing resolution returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`
   166→        );
   167→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);
   168→      } catch (error) {
   169→        // Re-throw HttpException immediately (e.g., parsing errors) - don't retry
   170→        if (error instanceof HttpException) {
   171→          throw error;
   172→        }
   173→
   174→        // Non-retryable HTTP error — surface immediately
   175→        if (error instanceof DelegatedRoutingHttpError) {
   176→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
   177→          throw new HttpException(
   178→            'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network',
   179→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   180→          );
   181→        }
   182→
   183→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
   184→
   185→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
   186→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   187→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   188→          this.logger.warn(
   189→            `IPNS resolve attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
   190→          );
   191→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   192→        }
   193→      }
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   197→    this.logger.error(
   198→      `Failed to resolve IPNS name after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`
   199→    );
   200→    throw new HttpException(
   201→      'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network after multiple attempts',
   202→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   203→    );
   204→  }
   205→
   206→  private async fetchWithTimeout<T>(
   207→    url: string,
   208→    init: RequestInit,
   209→    consume: (response: Response) => Promise<T>
   210→  ): Promise<T> {
   211→    const controller = new AbortController();
   212→    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), this.requestTimeoutMs);
   213→    try {
   214→      const response = await fetch(url, { ...init, signal: controller.signal });
   215→      return await consume(response);
   216→    } finally {
   217→      clearTimeout(timeout);
   218→    }
   219→  }
   220→
   221→  private getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter: string | null, attempt: number): number {
   222→    const fallback = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   223→    if (!retryAfter) return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   224→
   225→    const seconds = Number(retryAfter);
   226→    if (Number.isFinite(seconds) && seconds >= 0) {
   227→      return Math.min(seconds * 1000, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   228→    }
   229→
   230→    const retryAt = Date.parse(retryAfter);
   231→    if (!Number.isNaN(retryAt)) {
   232→      return Math.min(Math.max(0, retryAt - Date.now()), this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   233→    }
   234→
   235→    return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   236→  }
   237→
   238→  private delay(ms: number): Promise<void> {
   239→    return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
   240→  }
   241→}
   242→

errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(\n          url,\n          {\n            method: 'GET',\n            headers: {\n              Accept: 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',\n            },\n          },\n          async (response) => ({\n            ok: response.ok,\n            status: response.status,\n            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),\n            data: response.ok ? new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer()) : null,\n            errorText:\n              !response.ok && response.status !== 404 && response.status !== 429\n                ? await response.text()\n                : '',\n          })\n        );\n\n        // 404 means IPNS name not found - not an error\n        if (status === 404) {\n          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name not found via ${backend}: ${ipnsName}`);\n          this.metricsService.delegatedRoutingRequests.inc({\n            operation: 'resolve',\n            backend,\n            outcome: 'not_found',\n          });\n          return null;\n        }\n\n        if (ok) {\n          this.metricsService.delegatedRoutingRequests.inc({\n            operation: 'resolve',\n            backend,\n            outcome: 'success',\n          });\n          return data;\n        }\n\n        // Handle rate limiting\n        if (status === 429) {\n          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {\n            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);\n            this.logger.warn(\n              `Rate limited on IPNS resolve (${backend}), retrying in ${delayMs}ms`\n            );\n            await this.delay(delayMs);\n          }\n          continue;\n        }\n\n        // Non-retryable error\n        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client\n        this.logger.error(\n          `Delegated routing (${backend}) resolution returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`\n        );\n        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);\n      } catch (error) {\n        // Re-throw HttpException immediately (e.g., parsing errors) - don't retry\n        if (error instanceof HttpException) {\n          throw error;\n        }\n\n        // Non-retryable HTTP error — surface immediately\n        if (error instanceof DelegatedRoutingHttpError) {\n          this.metricsService.delegatedRoutingRequests.inc({\n            operation: 'resolve',\n            backend,\n            outcome: 'error',\n          });\n          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details\n          throw new HttpException(\n            'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network',\n            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY\n          );\n        }\n\n        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));\n\n        // Exponential backoff for network errors\n        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {\n          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);\n          this.logger.warn(\n            `IPNS resolve attempt ${attempt + 1} (${backend}) failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`\n          );\n          await this.delay(delayMs);\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message\n    this.logger.error(\n      `Failed to resolve IPNS name via ${backend} after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`\n    );\n    this.metricsService.delegatedRoutingRequests.inc({\n      operation: 'resolve',\n      backend,\n      outcome: 'error',\n    });\n    throw new HttpException(\n      'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network after multiple attempts',\n      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY\n    );\n  }\n\n  private async fetchWithTimeout<T>(\n    url: string,\n    init: RequestInit,\n    consume: (response: Response) => Promise<T>\n  ): Promise<T> {\n    const controller = new AbortController();\n    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), this.requestTimeoutMs);\n    try {\n      const response = await fetch(url, { ...init, signal: controller.signal });\n      return await consume(response);\n    } finally {\n      clearTimeout(timeout);\n    }\n  }\n\n  private getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter: string | null, attempt: number): number {\n    const fallback = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);\n    if (!retryAfter) return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);\n\n    const seconds = Number(retryAfter);\n    if (Number.isFinite(seconds) && seconds >= 0) {\n      return Math.min(seconds * 1000, this.maxRetryDelayMs);\n    }\n\n    const retryAt = Date.parse(retryAfter);\n    if (!Number.isNaN(retryAt)) {\n      return Math.min(Math.max(0, retryAt - Date.now()), this.maxRetryDelayMs);\n    }\n\n    return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);\n  }\n\n  private delay(ms: number): Promise<void> {\n    return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));\n  }\n}\n"
}

     9→function createMockResponse(
