```json
  "content": "import { HttpException, HttpStatus } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\nimport { DelegatedRoutingClient } from './delegated-routing.client';\nimport { MetricsService } from '../metrics/metrics.service';\n\n// Mock global fetch\nconst mockFetch = jest.fn();\nglobal.fetch = mockFetch;\n\nfunction createMockResponse(\n  status: number,\n  opts: { body?: string; buffer?: ArrayBuffer; headers?: Record<string, string> } = {}\n): Response {\n  const { body = '', buffer, headers = {} } = opts;\n  const headerMap = new Map(Object.entries(headers));\n  return {\n    ok: status >= 200 && status < 300,\n    status,\n    headers: { get: (key: string) => headerMap.get(key) ?? null },\n    text: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(body),\n    arrayBuffer: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(buffer ?? new ArrayBuffer(0)),\n  } as unknown as Response;\n}\n\nfunction createMockMetrics(): MetricsService {\n  return {\n    delegatedRoutingRequests: { inc: jest.fn() },\n    delegatedRoutingFallbacks: { inc: jest.fn() },\n  } as unknown as MetricsService;\n}\n\ndescribe('DelegatedRoutingClient', () => {\n  let client: DelegatedRoutingClient;\n  let configService: ConfigService;\n  let metricsService: MetricsService;\n  // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any\n  let delaySpy: jest.SpyInstance<Promise<void>, [ms: number], any>;\n\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    jest.restoreAllMocks();\n    mockFetch.mockReset();\n\n    configService = {\n      get: jest.fn((key: string, fallback: string) => {\n        if (key === 'DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL') return 'https://routing.example.com';\n        if (key === 'DELEGATED_ROUTING_FALLBACK_URL') return '';\n        return fallback;\n      }),\n    } as unknown as ConfigService;\n\n    metricsService = createMockMetrics();\n    client = new DelegatedRoutingClient(configService, metricsService);\n\n    // Eliminate real delays by making delay() resolve instantly\n    delaySpy = jest.spyOn(client as never, 'delay' as never).mockResolvedValue(undefined as never);\n  });\n\n  describe('constructor', () => {\n    it('reads DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL from config', () => {\n      expect(configService.get).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n        'DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL',\n        'https://delegated-ipfs.dev'\n      );\n    });\n\n    it('reads DELEGATED_ROUTING_FALLBACK_URL from config', () => {\n      expect(configService.get).toHaveBeenCalledWith('DELEGATED_ROUTING_FALLBACK_URL', '');\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('publish', () => {\n    const ipnsName = 'k51qzi5uqu5dg12345';\n    const recordBytes = new Uint8Array([1, 2, 3]);\n\n    it('publishes successfully on first attempt', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockResolvedValueOnce(createMockResponse(200));\n\n      await client.publish(ipnsName, recordBytes);\n\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n      const [url, init] = mockFetch.mock.calls[0];\n      expect(url).toBe(\n        `https://routing.example.com/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`\n      );\n      expect(init.method).toBe('PUT');\n      expect(init.headers['Content-Type']).toBe('application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record');\n    });\n\n    it('records success metric on primary', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockResolvedValueOnce(createMockResponse(200));\n\n      await client.publish(ipnsName, recordBytes);\n\n      expect(metricsService.delegatedRoutingRequests.inc).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n        operation: 'publish',\n        backend: 'primary',\n        outcome: 'success',\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('throws BAD_GATEWAY on non-retryable HTTP error', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockResolvedValue(createMockResponse(500, { body: 'Internal Server Error' }));\n\n      const error = await client.publish(ipnsName, recordBytes).catch((e) => e);\n\n      expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(HttpException);\n      expect(error.getStatus()).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);\n      // Should not retry — only one fetch call\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n    });\n\n    it('retries on network errors with exponential backoff', async () => {\n      mockFetch\n        .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('fetch failed'))\n        .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('fetch failed'))\n        .mockResolvedValueOnce(createMockResponse(200));\n\n      await client.publish(ipnsName, recordBytes);\n\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\n      // Delay called twice (attempts 0 and 1)\n      expect(delaySpy).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);\n      expect(delaySpy).toHaveBeenNthCalledWith(1, 1000); // 1000 * 2^0\n      expect(delaySpy).toHaveBeenNthCalledWith(2, 2000); // 1000 * 2^1\n    });\n\n    it('throws BAD_GATEWAY after exhausting retries on network errors', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockRejectedValue(new Error('fetch failed'));\n\n      const error = await client.publish(ipnsName, recordBytes).catch((e) => e);\n\n      expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(HttpException);\n      expect(error.getStatus()).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\n      // Delay called for attempts 0 and 1, not for last attempt\n      expect(delaySpy).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);\n    });\n\n    it('retries on 429 with Retry-After header', async () => {\n      mockFetch\n        .mockResolvedValueOnce(createMockResponse(429, { headers: { 'Retry-After': '2' } }))\n        .mockResolvedValueOnce(createMockResponse(200));\n\n      await client.publish(ipnsName, recordBytes);\n\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);\n      expect(delaySpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(2000); // 2 seconds from Retry-After\n    });\n\n    it('skips sleeping on 429 on last retry attempt', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockResolvedValue(createMockResponse(429, { headers: { 'Retry-After': '5' } }));\n\n      const error = await client.publish(ipnsName, recordBytes).catch((e) => e);\n\n      expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(HttpException);\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\n      // Should only delay for first 2 attempts, skip delay on last\n      expect(delaySpy).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);\n    });\n\n    it('uses abort signal for timeout', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockResolvedValueOnce(createMockResponse(200));\n\n      await headers: { get: () => null },\n        arrayBuffer: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(testBuffer),\n      } as unknown as Response;\n    }\n\n    it('resolves successfully and returns Uint8Array', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockResolvedValueOnce(okResponse());\n\n      const result = await client.resolve(ipnsName);\n\n      expect(result).toBeInstanceOf(Uint8Array);\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n      const [url, init] = mockFetch.mock.calls[0];\n      expect(url).toBe(\n expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n    });\n\n    it('throws BAD_GATEWAY on non-retryable HTTP error', async () => {\n      mockFetch.mockResolvedValue(createMockResponse(500, { body: expect(error.getStatus()).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n    });\n\n    it('retries on network errors', async () => {\n      mockFetch\n expect(result).toBeInstanceOf(Uint8Array);\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);\n      expect(delaySpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(1000);\n    });\n\n    it('retries on 429 with Retry-After', expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(HttpException);\n      expect(error.getStatus()).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);\n      expect(mockFetch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('fallback });\n\n    it('records fallback metric when primary fails', async () => {\n      mockFetch\n        .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('ECONNREFUSED'))\n        .mockRejectedValueOnce(new expect(fallbackMetrics.delegatedRoutingRequests.inc).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n        operation: 'publish',\n        backend: 'fallback',\n        outcome: 'success',\n      });\n    });\n\n > AGENT
```

Done. Here's what was implemented:

**API-level fallback (`DelegatedRoutingClient`):**
- `DELEGATED_ROUTING_FALLBACK_URL` env var — set to `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` on staging
- Primary (someguy) is tried first with full retry logic (3 attempts + exponential backoff)
- Only if primary exhausts all retries, fallback URL is tried with the same retry logic
- No fallback configured = same behavior as before

**Prometheus metrics (two new counters):**
- `cipherbox_delegated_routing_requests_total{operation, backend, outcome}` — every request tagged with `primary`/`fallback` backend and `success`/`error`/`not_found` outcome
- `cipherbox_delegated_routing_fallbacks_total{operation}` — incremented each time primary fails and fallback is triggered

**Grafana alerting you can set up:**
- Alert on `rate(cipherbox_delegated_routing_fallbacks_total[5m]) > 0` — any fallback usage
- Dashboard panel: `sum by (backend) (rate(cipherbox_delegated_routing_requests_total[5m]))` — shows primary vs fallback request rates
- Ratio: `fallbacks / total requests` gives you the health signal for someguy

**Tests:** 27 unit tests pass (including 6 new fallback-specific tests), plus all 58 IPNS service tests and 31 republish tests still green.

```
     1→import {

    10→} from '@nestjs/common';
    11→import { InjectRepository } from '@nestjs/typeorm';
    12→import { Repository } from 'typeorm';
    13→import { FolderIpns } from './entities/folder-ipns.entity';
    14→import {
    15→  PublishIpnsDto,
    16→  PublishIpnsEntryDto,
    17→  PublishIpnsResponseDto,
    18→  BatchPublishIpnsDto,
    19→  BatchPublishIpnsResponseDto,
    20→} from './dto';
    21→import { RepublishService } from '../republish/republish.service';
    22→import { DelegatedRoutingClient } from './delegated-routing.client';
    23→import { MetricsService } from '../metrics/metrics.service';
    24→import { parseIpnsRecord } from './ipns-record-parser';
    25→
    26→@Injectable()
    27→export class IpnsService {
    28→  private readonly logger = new Logger(IpnsService.name);
    29→
    30→  constructor(
    31→    @InjectRepository(FolderIpns)
    32→    private readonly folderIpnsRepository: Repository<FolderIpns>,
    33→    private readonly delegatedRouting: DelegatedRoutingClient,
    34→    @Inject(forwardRef(() => RepublishService))
    35→    private readonly republishService: RepublishService,
    36→    private readonly metricsService: MetricsService
    37→  ) {}
    38→
    39→  /**
    40→   * Publish a pre-signed IPNS record to the IPFS network via delegated routing
    41→   * and track the folder in the database for TEE republishing
    42→   */
    43→  async publishRecord(
    44→    userId: string,
    45→    dto: PublishIpnsDto | PublishIpnsEntryDto,
    46→    recordType: 'folder' | 'file' = 'folder'
    47→  ): Promise<PublishIpnsResponseDto> {
    48→    const endTimer = this.metricsService.ipfsIpnsDuration.startTimer({
    49→      operation: 'publish',
    50→      source: '',
    51→    });
    52→    let result = 'success';
    53→
    54→    try {
    55→      // Validate base64 record
    56→      let recordBytes: Uint8Array;
    57→      try {
    58→        recordBytes = Uint8Array.from(atob(dto.record), (c) => c.charCodeAt(0));
    59→      } catch {
    60→        throw new BadRequestException('Invalid base64-encoded record');
    61→      }
    62→
    63→      // Save to DB first so resolve always has a fallback, even if delegated
    64→      // routing fails (e.g. rate-limited, network error, DHT propagation delay).
    65→      const folder = await this.upsertFolderIpns(
    66→        userId,
    67→        dto.ipnsName,
    68→        dto.metadataCid,
    69→        dto.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
    70→        dto.keyEpoch,
    71→        recordType,
    72→        dto.expectedSequenceNumber
    73→      );
    74→
    75→      // Publish to delegated routing API (fire-and-forget — DB is the reliable source).
    76→      // DHT propagation via someguy takes ~10-30s per record. Since the DB record
    77→      // is already saved and resolveRecord() always checks/prefers DB data, there is
    78→      // no need to block the API response on DHT propagation. Metrics are still
    79→      // collected via the detached promise chain (catch + then).
    80→      const publishStart = process.hrtime.bigint();
    81→      this.delegatedRouting
    82→        .publish(dto.ipnsName, recordBytes)
    83→        .catch((error) => {
    84→          this.logger.warn(
    85→            `Delegated routing publish failed for ${dto.ipnsName}, DB record saved: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`
    86→          );
    87→          return 'error' as const;
    88→        })
    89→        .then((outcome) => {
    90→          const publishElapsed = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - publishStart) / 1e9;
    91→          this.metricsService.ipnsPublishDuration.observe(
    92→            { outcome: outcome === 'error' ? 'error' : 'success' },
    93→            publishElapsed
    94→          );
    95→        })
    96→        .catch((error) => {
    97→          this.logger.error(
    98→            `Failed to record IPNS publish metrics for ${dto.ipnsName}: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`
    99→          );
   100→        });
   101→
   102→      return {
   103→        success: true,
   104→        ipnsName: dto.ipnsName,
   105→        sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
   106→      };
   107→    } catch (error) {
   108→      result = 'error';
   109→      throw error;
   110→    } finally {
   111→      endTimer({ result });
   112→    }
   113→  }
   114→
   115→  /**
   116→   * Batch publish multiple IPNS records with concurrency-limited processing.
   117→   * Supports partial success: individual record failures do not fail the batch.
   118→   * Processes up to 10 records concurrently.
   119→   */
   120→  async publishBatch(
   121→    userId: string,
   122→    dto: BatchPublishIpnsDto
   123→  ): Promise<BatchPublishIpnsResponseDto> {
   124→    const results: PublishIpnsResponseDto[] = [];
   125→    let totalSucceeded = 0;
   126→    let totalFailed = 0;
   127→
   128→    const CONCURRENCY = 10;
   129→
   130→    // Process records in batches of CONCURRENCY, delegating to publishRecord
   131→    for (let i = 0; i < dto.records.length; i += CONCURRENCY) {
   132→      const batch = dto.records.slice(i, i + CONCURRENCY);
   133→
   134→      const settled = await Promise.allSettled(
   135→        batch.map((entry) => this.publishRecord(userId, entry, entry.recordType ?? 'folder'))
   136→      );
   137→
   138→      for (let j = 0; j < settled.length; j++) {
   139→        const result = settled[j];
   140→        if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {
   141→          results.push(result.value);
   142→          totalSucceeded++;
   143→        } else {
   144→          const reason = result.reason;
   145→          // If any record (especially a folder record) has a conflict, fail the entire batch
   146→          if (reason instanceof ConflictException) {
   147→            throw reason;
   148→          }
   149→          const ipnsName = batch[j]?.ipnsName ?? 'unknown';
   150→          this.logger.warn(
   151→            `Batch publish failed for ${ipnsName}: ${reason instanceof Error ? reason.message : String(reason)}`
   152→          );
   153→          results.push({
   154→            success: false,
   155→            ipnsName,
   156→            sequenceNumber: '0',
   157→          });
   158→          totalFailed++;
   159→        }
   160→      }
   161→    }
   162→
   163→    return { results, totalSucceeded, totalFailed };
   164→  }
   165→
   166→  /**
   167→   * Create or update a folder/file IPNS entry.
   168→   * Handles both folder metadata and per-file metadata IPNS records.
   169→   */
   170→  private async upsertFolderIpns(
   171→    userId: string,
   172→    ipnsName: string,
   173→    metadataCid: string,
   174→    encryptedIpnsPrivateKey?: string,
   175→    keyEpoch?: number,
   176→    recordType: 'folder' | 'file' = 'folder',
   177→    expectedSequenceNumber?: string
   178→  ): Promise<FolderIpns> {
   179→    const existing = await this.getFolderIpns(userId, ipnsName);
   180→
   181→    // Conflict detection: when expectedSequenceNumber is provided, verify it matches
   182→    if (existing && expectedSequenceNumber !== undefined) {
   183→      const expected = BigInt(expectedSequenceNumber);
   184→      const current = BigInt(existing.sequenceNumber);
   185→      if (expected !== current) {
   186→        throw new ConflictException({
   187→          statusCode: 409,
   188→          message: 'Sequence number mismatch: folder was modified by another device',
   189→          currentSequenceNumber: existing.sequenceNumber,
   190→          expectedSequenceNumber,
   191→        });
   192→      }
   193→    }
   194→
   195→    if (existing) {
   196→      // Update existing entry
   197→      existing.latestCid = metadataCid;
   198→      existing.sequenceNumber = (BigInt(existing.sequenceNumber) + 1n).toString();
   199→      existing.recordType = recordType;
   200→      existing.updatedAt = new Date();
   201→
   202→      // Only update encrypted key if provided (e.g., on key rotation)
   203→      if (encryptedIpnsPrivateKey && keyEpoch !== undefined) {
   204→        existing.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey = Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex');
   205→        existing.keyEpoch = keyEpoch;
   206→      }
   207→
   208→      const saved = await this.folderIpnsRepository.save(existing);
   209→
   210→      // Auto-enroll for TEE republishing when encrypted key is provided
   211→      if (encryptedIpnsPrivateKey && keyEpoch !== undefined) {
   212→        this.republishService
   213→          .enrollFolder(
   214→            userId,
   215→            ipnsName,
   216→            Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex'),
   217→            keyEpoch,
   218→            metadataCid,
   219→            saved.sequenceNumber
   220→          )
   221→          .catch((err) =>
   222→            this.logger.warn(
   223→              `Failed to enroll ${recordType} ${ipnsName} for republishing: ${err.message}`
   224→            )
   225→          );
   226→      }
   227→
   228→      return saved;
   229→    }
   230→
   231→    // Create new entry — sequence starts at '1' to match the IPNS record
   232→    // the client signed (clients compute newSeq = 0n + 1n = 1n for first publish).
   233→    const folder = this.folderIpnsRepository.create({
   234→      userId,
   235→      ipnsName,
   236→      latestCid: metadataCid,
   237→      sequenceNumber: '1',
   238→      encryptedIpnsPrivateKey: encryptedIpnsPrivateKey
   239→        ? Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex')
   240→        : null,
   241→      keyEpoch: keyEpoch ?? null,
   242→      isRoot: false, // Root folder is tracked in Vault entity
   243→      recordType,
   244→    });
   245→
   246→    const saved = await this.folderIpnsRepository.save(folder);
   247→
   248→    // Auto-enroll for TEE republishing when encrypted key is provided
   249→    if (encryptedIpnsPrivateKey && keyEpoch !== undefined) {
   250→      this.republishService
   251→        .enrollFolder(
   252→          userId,
   253→          ipnsName,
   254→          Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex'),
   255→          keyEpoch,
   256→          metadataCid,
   257→          saved.sequenceNumber
   258→        )
   259→        .catch((err) =>
   260→          this.logger.warn(
   261→            `Failed to enroll ${recordType} ${ipnsName} for republishing: ${err.message}`
   262→          )
   263→        );
   264→    }
   265→
   266→    return saved;
   267→  }
   268→
   269→  /**
   270→   * Get a folder IPNS entry by user and IPNS name
   271→   */
   272→  async getFolderIpns(userId: string, ipnsName: string): Promise<FolderIpns | null> {
   273→    return this.folderIpnsRepository.findOne({
   274→      where: { userId, ipnsName },
   275→    });
   276→  }
   277→
   278→  /**
   279→   * Get all folder IPNS entries for a user (for TEE republishing)
   280→   */
   281→  async getAllFolderIpns(userId: string): Promise<FolderIpns[]> {
   282→    return this.folderIpnsRepository.find({
   283→      where: { userId },
   284→      order: { createdAt: 'ASC' },
   285→    });
   286→  }
   287→
   288→  /**
   289→   * Resolve an IPNS name to its current CID via delegated routing,
   290→   * falling back to the DB-cached CID when delegated routing is unavailable
   291→   * or when the record is not found in the DHT.
   292→   * Returns null if the IPNS name is not found anywhere (404)
   293→   */
   294→  async resolveRecord(ipnsName: string): Promise<{
   295→    cid: string;
   296→    sequenceNumber: string;
   297→    signatureV2?: string;
   298→    data?: string;
   299→    pubKey?: string;
   300→  } | null> {
   301→    const endTimer = this.metricsService.ipfsIpnsDuration.startTimer({
   302→      operation: 'resolve',
   303→    });
   304→    let timerResult = 'success';
   305→    let timerSource = 'network';
   306→    const startTime = process.hrtime.bigint();
   307→    let source = 'network';
   308→    let resolveFound = false;
   309→
   310→    try {
   311→      let result: {
   312→        cid: string;
   313→        sequenceNumber: string;
   314→        signatureV2?: string;
   315→        data?: string;
   316→        pubKey?: string;
   317→      } | null = null;
   318→
   319→      try {
   320→        const recordBytes = await this.delegatedRouting.resolve(ipnsName);
   321→        if (recordBytes) {
   322→          result = this.parseIpnsRecordBytes(recordBytes);
   323→          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name resolved successfully: ${ipnsName} -> ${result.cid}`);
   324→        }
   325→      } catch (error) {
   326→        // Fall back to DB cache on BAD_GATEWAY (delegated routing failures)
   327→        if (error instanceof HttpException && error.getStatus() === HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY) {
   328→          this.logger.warn(`Delegated routing failed for ${ipnsName}, falling back to DB cache`);
   329→          timerResult = 'error';
   330→          source = 'db_cache';
   331→        } else {
   332→          timerResult = 'error';
   333→          throw error;
   334→        }
   335→      }
   336→
   337→      // Always check DB cache — it's written synchronously during publish
   338→      // and may be ahead of the network (delegated routing can serve stale records)
   339→      const cached = await this.folderIpnsRepository.findOne({
   340→        where: { ipnsName },
   341→      });
   342→
   343→      if (result && cached?.latestCid) {
   344→        // Both sources available — prefer the one with the higher sequence number
   345→        const networkSeq = BigInt(result.sequenceNumber);
   346→        const dbSeq = BigInt(cached.sequenceNumber);
   347→        if (dbSeq > networkSeq) {
   348→          this.logger.log(
   349→            `DB cache has newer sequence (${dbSeq} > ${networkSeq}) for ${ipnsName}, using DB: ${cached.latestCid}`
   350→          );
   351→          timerSource = 'db';
   352→          source = 'network_stale';
   353→          resolveFound = true;
   354→          return { cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };
   355→        }
   356→        timerSource = 'network';
   357→        resolveFound = true;
   358→        return result;
   359→      }
   360→
   361→      if (result) {
   362→        timerSource = 'network';
   363→        resolveFound = true;
   364→        return result;
   365→      }
   366→
   367→      // Delegated routing returned null (404) or threw BAD_GATEWAY — try DB cache
   368→      if (cached?.latestCid) {
   369→        this.logger.log(`Resolved ${ipnsName} from DB cache: ${cached.latestCid}`);
   370→        timerSource = 'db';
   371→        source = 'db_cache';
   372→        resolveFound = true;
   373→        return { cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };
   374→      }
   375→
   376→      return null;
   377→    } catch (error) {
   378→      timerResult = 'error';
   379→      throw error;
   380→    } finally {
   381→      endTimer({ result: timerResult, source: timerSource });
   382→      if (resolveFound) {
   383→        const elapsed = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - startTime) / 1e9;
   384→        this.metricsService.ipnsResolveDuration.observe({ source, outcome: timerResult }, elapsed);
   385→      }
   386→    }
   387→  }
   388→
   389→  /**
   390→   * Parse an IPNS record to extract CID and sequence number
   391→   * Uses inline protobuf decoder — no external dependencies
   392→   */
   393→  private parseIpnsRecordBytes(recordBytes: Uint8Array): {
   394→    cid: string;
   395→    sequenceNumber: string;
   396→    signatureV2?: string;
   397→    data?: string;
   398→    pubKey?: string;
   399→  } {
   400→    try {
   401→      const record = parseIpnsRecord(recordBytes);
   402→
   403→      // Extract CID from the Value field (format: /ipfs/<cid>)
   404→      const valuePath = record.value;
   405→      const cidMatch = valuePath.match(/\/ipfs\/([a-zA-Z0-9]+)/);
   406→      if (!cidMatch) {
   407→        this.logger.error('Failed to extract CID from IPNS record value');
   408→        throw new HttpException('Invalid IPNS record format', HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);
   409→      }
   410→
   411→      const cid = cidMatch[1];
   412→      const sequenceNumber = String(record.sequence ?? 0n);
   413→
   414→      // Base64-encode signature fields if present
   415→      const signatureV2 = record.signatureV2
   416→        ? Buffer.from(record.signatureV2).toString('base64')
   417→        : undefined;
   418→      const data = record.data ? Buffer.from(record.data).toString('base64') : undefined;
   419→      const pubKey = record.pubKey ? Buffer.from(record.pubKey).toString('base64') : undefined;
   420→
   421→      this.logger.debug(`Parsed IPNS record: cid=${cid}, sequenceNumber=${sequenceNumber}`);
   422→      return { cid, sequenceNumber, signatureV2, data, pubKey };
   423→    } catch (error) {
   424→      if (error instanceof HttpException) {
   425→        throw error;
   426→      }
   427→      this.logger.error(`Failed to parse IPNS record: ${error}`);
   428→      throw new HttpException('Invalid IPNS record format', HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY);
   429→    }
   430→  }
   431→}
   432→
```

> AGENT

Now I see the issue clearly. Look at the resolve flow in `resolveRecord()` (line 294):

1. **Line 320**: Try delegated routing (someguy) first
2. **Line 339**: **Always** query the DB, regardless of whether network resolved
3. **Lines 343-358**: If both exist, prefer whichever has the **higher sequence number** — and since DB is written synchronously during publish while DHT propagation takes 10-30s, the **DB always wins**
4. **Lines 367-373**: If network returned null/404, use DB cache

The problem is line 75 in `publishRecord()`:

```
// Publish to delegated routing API (fire-and-forget — DB is the reliable source).
```

Publishing to someguy is **fire-and-forget** (detached promise). The DB is always ahead because it's written synchronously before the DHT publish even starts. So every resolve hits the DB with a higher sequence number and the network result is discarded.

**With a working someguy sidecar, this architecture still guarantees 100% DB resolves.** The someguy fix alone doesn't change this — the resolve logic is designed to prefer the DB.

To actually shift resolution toward the network, the resolve logic would need to change. But that's a deliberate architectural choice — the DB-first approach exists because:
1. DHT propagation is slow (10-30s) and clients need instant reads-after-writes
2. The DB is the only source that's always up-to-date immediately after a publish
3. The network resolve exists as a **cross-check**, not as the primary source

That said, the someguy sidecar still provides real value even if the DB handles 100% of resolves:
- **TEE republishing** uses someguy to publish records to the DHT so the recovery tool can resolve without the API
- **The standalone recovery tool** resolves via someguy/DHT, not the API — it's the disaster recovery path
- **Third-party IPNS consumers** (anyone resolving your IPNS names outside CipherBox) benefit from DHT records

Would you like me to change the resolve strategy to prefer network when the sequence numbers match (instead of always preferring DB), or is the current DB-first approach correct for your use case?

> DEVELOPER

I guess until we start running in to database issues, its not too serious a problem.

> AGENT

Agreed. The DB-first approach is the right call for now — it gives instant read-after-write consistency which matters more than reducing DB load. The someguy sidecar earns its keep through TEE republishing and the recovery tool path, which is the real reason Phase 19 existed.

If DB load ever becomes a concern, the lever to pull is straightforward: when network and DB sequence numbers match, return the network result instead of re-querying the DB. But that's a future optimization, not a current need.

Shall I merge the PR, or is there anything else you'd like added to it?

> AGENT

You're right that it worked locally — that's because Docker healthchecks with `CMD-SHELL` run **inside the container**, but when the container network is in `host` mode or the check happens to work through docker exec with the host shell, `wget` from the host can satisfy it. On staging with proper container isolation, it would fail since the someguy image is based on a minimal Go distroless/alpine that ships `curl` but not `wget`.

That said, let me actually verify this claim before we push — CodeRabbit's learnings could be wrong.

> AGENT

That was the commit that already completed and was pushed. All review comments are resolved and the PR is ready.

> DEVELOPER

since we are now running someguy, is there any real benefit to the mock-ipns-routing ? can the someguy container not just get used for all dev routing needs?

> AGENT

Good question. The mock still earns its keep for **CI** — all the workflow files (`ci.yml`, `e2e.yml`, `load-test.yml`, `desktop-e2e.yml`) point at `localhost:3001`. In CI, someguy would need minutes of DHT warm-up and outbound internet access to bootstrap peers, which makes tests slow and non-deterministic. The mock is instant and has zero external dependencies.

For **local dev** though, you're right — if someguy is already running on your Docker host, there's no reason to also run the mock. You just point `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` at someguy. The mock becomes redundant unless you specifically want offline/isolated testing.

So the mock stays in CI workflows, but we could remove it from the local `docker-compose.yml` since someguy is there now. Or just leave both and let the `.env` decide which one to use — which is effectively what we have today. Your call on whether the cleanup is worth a change.

> DEVELOPER

ok that makes sense, think I will leave it for now.

> AGENT

Sounds good. The PR is ready at https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/325 whenever you want to merge it.

> DEVELOPER

looks like coderabbit is 

`myankelev@Michaels-MacBook-Pro ~ % docker exec cipherbox-someguy which wget; docker exec cipherbox-someguy which curl   /usr/bin/curl`

> AGENT

So the someguy image has `curl` but **not** `wget`. CodeRabbit was right — the `wget` healthcheck would have failed inside the container. Good thing we switched it.

The reason it appeared to work locally is likely that Docker was reporting the container as healthy based on the exit code before the healthcheck actually ran inside the container, or the start period hadn't elapsed yet.

The `curl -sf` change stays. PR is clean with all review comments resolved.

> AGENT

There's the problem:

```
⚠ There are untagged, merged release PRs outstanding - aborting
```

Release-please found that PR #323 (the release PR) was merged but never got its `v0.26.2` tag created. This creates a deadlock — release-please won't create new release PRs or tags until the outstanding one is resolved.

This usually happens when the release-please workflow that ran on the merge of PR #323 didn't have permission to create the tag, or the tag creation step failed silently. The run ID `23419093591` is the one triggered by the merge of #323 itself, and it aborted because it saw itself as an untagged merged release PR.

The fix is to manually create the `v0.26.2` tag on the merge commit of PR #323:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01BDJxkFTTiVRo3ZuXStYWbK
```json
{
  "command": "gh pr view 323 --json mergeCommit --jq '.mergeCommit.oid'",
  "description": "Get merge commit of release PR #323"
}
```

> AGENT

So here's the full chain of events:

1. **Release PR #323 merged** → release-please ran but couldn't create `v0.26.2` tag (the "untagged merged release PRs" deadlock issue)
2. **I pushed `v0.26.2-staging-rc-1` tag** → `deploy-staging` triggered immediately on the tag push event
3. **`tag-staging-release` also ran** (via workflow_dispatch) but failed because it validates that the `v0.26.2` release tag exists first — which it doesn't, because release-please never created it

The root issue is that `deploy-staging` doesn't gate on `tag-staging-release` — it triggers directly on the tag push. And the staging deploy is now running against code that hasn't been officially released/tagged by release-please.

> AGENT

Found it. The PR title is `chore: release main` but the config expects `chore: release v${version}`:

```
pull-request-title-pattern: "chore: release v${version}"
```

When release-please runs post-merge, it tries to match the title against this pattern to extract the version and create the tag. `chore: release main` doesn't match `chore: release v${version}`, so it can't determine which version to tag.

```
name: Release Please

on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main

permissions:
  contents: write
  pull-requests: write

jobs:
  release-please:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/create-github-app-token@v1
        id: app-token
        with:
          app-id: ${{ vars.RELEASE_BOT_APP_ID }}
          private-key: ${{ secrets.RELEASE_BOT_PRIVATE_KEY }}

      - uses: googleapis/release-please-action@v4
        with:
          config-file: release-please-config.json
          manifest-file: .release-please-manifest.json
          token: ${{ steps.app-token.outputs.token }}
```

> AGENT

Got it — v0.26.0 was created manually, not by release-please. So the pattern is consistent: **release-please has never successfully auto-tagged since the reconfiguration**. The `chore: release main` title doesn't match the `chore: release v${version}` pattern, so it can't extract the version from the title to create the tag.

The fix is to align the `pull-request-title-pattern` with what release-please actually generates. With the Merge plugin (multiple packages), release-please produces `chore: release main` — it can't put a single version number in the title because there could be multiple versions.

> AGENT

There it is clearly:

```
⚠ PR component: undefined does not match configured component: cipher-box
⚠ pullRequestTitlePattern miss the part of '${version}'
```

Release-please can't extract `${version}` or `${component}` from `chore: release main`. The Merge plugin combines all component releases into one PR and uses a static title that doesn't include version/component info.

The fix: change the `pull-request-title-pattern` to `chore: release main` — matching exactly what the Merge plugin produces. Release-please will then use the PR body (which contains the version details) to determine what to tag.

> AGENT

Only #323 is stuck with `autorelease: pending`. But the log said "Expected 5 releases, only found 4" and "Missing 1 paths: `.`" — meaning it's looking for a pre-existing `v0.26.1` tag (for the root package) that was supposed to have been created by a **previous** run and wasn't.

So the sequence is: PR #323 contains releases for 5 packages (root + 4 sub-packages). When the sub-packages' releases were created (by #319), the root package's release was NOT created. Now #323 tries to reconcile and finds 4 tagged releases but the root `.` path is missing, so it can't proceed.

> AGENT

No `v0.26.1` tags at all — not for root, not for any sub-package. And the manifest says `0.26.1` for root but the sub-packages are still at `0.26.0`:

```json
".": "0.26.1",
"packages/core": "0.25.0",
"packages/api-client": "0.26.0",
"packages/sdk-core": "0.26.0",
"packages/sdk": "0.26.0"
```

So PR #323 only bumped the root package to `0.26.1`. Release-please is trying to create a release for the root (`.`) at `v0.26.1` but something prevents it, and since it can't complete, it aborts with the "untagged merged release PRs" error.

The immediate fix to unblock things: manually create the `v0.26.1` tag on the PR #323 merge commit and flip the label from `autorelease: pending` to `autorelease: tagged`. That clears the blockage so the next release-please run can proceed normally.

But the **root cause** is why release-please can't create the root tag itself. Want me to pull the full unfiltered log between "Found pull request #323" and "aborting" so we can see exactly where it fails?

It scanned all GitHub Releases and found tags for 4 sub-packages but **no release for path `.`** (the root). The `v0.26.0` tag exists but release-please can't match it to the root package — likely because `v0.26.0` was created manually and doesn't have the metadata release-please expects.

**Line 267** — The PR-building phase (second pass):
```
✔ No latest release found for path: ., component: , but a previous version (0.26.1) was specified in the manifest.
```
It can't find ANY release for the root package. So it walks **all 320+ commits back to the initial commit**, scanning every single one. It then tries to build a new release PR with 656 commits for path `.`.

**Lines 348-349**:
```
❯ commits: 656
✔ Considering: 656 commits
```

**Line 383-386** — Then the Merge plugin runs:
```
❯ running plugin: Merge
✔ Merging 1 pull requests
❯ Found pull request #323: 'chore: release main'
⚠ There are untagged, merged release PRs outstanding - aborting
```

The Merge plugin finds PR #323 still pending, so it aborts to avoid creating a duplicate.

**Root cause**: Release-please has **never** had a valid release for the root path (`.`). Every `v*` release was created manually, so release-please doesn't recognize them. Without a "latest release" anchor for path `.`, it walks the entire commit history every time, can't create the tag, and deadlocks.

To your question about `packages/crypto` — it's not in the config because it was removed in PR #316 (`fix(ci): remove packages/crypto from release-please extra-files`). It's presumably either deleted or merged into another package.

The real fix needs to give release-please a valid anchor release for path `.`. Want me to investigate whether creating a proper GitHub Release for `v0.26.1` (with the right metadata) via `gh release create` would unblock this?

> AGENT

It fetched 5 pages of releases (with the `release search depth: 400`) and found the 4 sub-package releases on the first page, then paginated through everything else but **never logged finding v0.26.0 for path `.`**. 

The `v0.26.0` release is there in the GitHub API, but release-please skipped it. The staging releases (`v0.26.0-staging-rc-1`, `v0.26.2-staging-rc-1`) appear before `v0.26.0` in the release list and their tags match the root package's `v{version}` pattern. Release-please likely matched one of those to path `.` first, then rejected it due to version mismatch, and marked path `.` as "already processed" so `v0.26.0` was skipped.

**The root cause: staging pre-release tags (`v*-staging*`) collide with the root package's tag pattern (`v{version}`).**

The fix: the root package needs `include-component-in-tag: true` with a distinct component name, OR the staging tags need a different format that doesn't match `v{version}` (e.g., `staging-v0.26.0-rc-1` instead of `v0.26.0-staging-rc-1`).

Which approach do you prefer? Changing the staging tag format is simpler and doesn't affect release-please config, but it means updating the deploy workflow trigger pattern and your tag naming convention.

> DEVELOPER

ok i think the staging tag format is probably easiest to deal with

> AGENT

Agreed. Let me find all the places that reference the staging tag pattern and update them.

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. The new format should be `staging-v0.26.2-rc-1` instead of `v0.26.2-staging-rc-1`. This way it won't start with `v` and release-please won't match it to the root package.

Changes needed:
1. `deploy-staging.yml` line 6: tag trigger pattern
2. `tag-staging.yml` line 66,72: RC tag construction  
3. `release-gate.yml` line 42: the grep filter (currently excludes `staging` — still works)
4. `CLAUDE.md`: documentation
5. Clean up the broken state: delete the staging pre-releases that are confusing release-please, fix PR #323's label

The `rc-N` suffix is a sequential counter allowing multiple staging deploys per version.

```
     3→## CRITICAL: Branch Protection & Signing Trust
     4→- **NEVER commit directly to `main`** — no exceptions, not even docs/todo moves
     5→- Commit signing requirement **disabled** on branch protection (2026-02-13) — GSD agent subprocesses can't access 1Password SSH agent
     6→- Tag protection still requires signatures; GitHub signs merge/release commits automatically
     7→- Git config `commit.gpgsign=true` still set — signing happens when 1Password agent is available
     8→- **Do NOT intentionally bypass signing** — no `-c commit.gpgsign=false`, no `--no-gpg-sign`
     9→- Unsigned commits from GSD agents are acceptable since PRs are squash-merged by GitHub with its own signature
    10→- **First action when starting ANY task:** determine the feature branch name, then switch to it
    11→  - If on `main`: `git checkout -b feat/<name>` immediately, before ANY other work
    12→  - If on a feature branch: verify it's the RIGHT branch for this task
    13→  - If on the WRONG feature branch: `git checkout main && git pull && git checkout -b feat/<new-name>`
    14→- Always branch off from latest `main` (`git pull` first)
    15→- **Before pushing follow-up commits to a branch**, check if the PR was already merged: `gh pr list --head <branch> --state merged`
    16→  - CI workflow fixes often get merged quickly, so always verify before pushing additional commits
    17→  - If merged: `git checkout main && git pull && git checkout -b fix/<new-name>` and cherry-pick or redo the change
    18→- The todo-move step in /gsd:check-todos must also happen on the feature branch, not main
    19→- This rule applies to ALL commit types: code, docs, config, todo moves, state updates — everything

    37→## Known Bugs (discovered Phase 7.1 UAT)

    39→- **Auth refresh race:** FIXED in PR #58

    42→## Parallel Agent Work
    43→- **Use git worktrees for parallel agents**, not branch switching on a shared working tree
    44→- Always commit lockfile changes when adding dev dependencies (CI uses `--frozen-lockfile`)

    46→## Phase 9 Desktop Client — UAT Progress
    47→- **Branch:** `feat/phase-9-desktop-client`
    48→- **Tests 1-14 passed**, Test 2 has open issue (tray status flaky), Test 15 pending
    49→- **Currently testing:** Test 15 (Quit unmounts cleanly)
    50→- **16 fixes applied** (9 from previous session + 7 this session)

    52→
    53→## FUSE-T Key Lessons
    54→- **SWITCHED TO SMB BACKEND**: NFS backend has unfixable macOS kernel bug — WRITE RPCs never reach FUSE-T for new files. SMB backend works correctly. Mount option: `backend=smb`.
    55→- **Vendored fuser with socket-read patch**: FUSE-T communicates via Unix domain socket, not /dev/fuse. Stock fuser does single read() expecting atomic messages, but sockets fragment large messages and coalesce small ones. Fix: peek at 4-byte header length, then loop-read exactly that many bytes. Patch in `vendor/fuser/src/channel.rs`.
    56→- **SMB opendir must return non-zero fh**: SMB backend treats `fh=0` as invalid handle. Use `next_fh.fetch_add(1)`.
    57→- **SMB rename FIXED**: Was failing with EPERM because `access(W_OK)` checked `req.uid() != attr.uid` — under SMB, UIDs differ. Fix: `access()` always grants (encryption is access control). UID in `create()`/`mkdir()` unified to `getuid()`.
    58→- **FUSE-T truncates filenames in rename callback** by 8 bytes — need suffix-match fallback
    59→- **NFS single-thread**: ALL callbacks on one thread. ANY blocking call stalls everything.
    60→- **NEVER block in read()**: Even 500ms blocks cause Finder "connection lost". Use channel-based prefetch.
    61→- **readdir/lookup consistency is critical**: NFS client does LOOKUP for every readdir entry including ".." — ENOENT causes disconnect
    62→- **NFS inode stability is critical**: populate_folder MUST reuse existing inode numbers. Allocating new inos for same-name children causes NFS "stale file handle" disconnects.
    63→- **Background refresh race**: async metadata refresh can overwrite local mutations. Use mutation cooldown (30s).
    64→- **Deduplicate readdir refreshes**: NFS calls readdir at offset=0 and offset=N — only fire refresh on offset=0.
    65→- **macOS keychain**: `keyring::set_password()` fails if item exists — always delete first. Debug builds skip Keychain entirely (each rebuild = new binary signature = repeated prompts).
    66→- **Stale mount cleanup**: After crash, ~/CipherBox may contain .DS_Store. App should rm contents before mount.
    67→- **Force unmount**: `umount` fails when Finder has handles open. Use `diskutil unmount force` as fallback.
    68→- **FUSE-T logs**: Check `~/Library/Logs/fuse-t/fuse-t.log` for NFS/SMB-level errors.
    69→
    70→## Tauri Webview Lessons
    71→- **Web3Auth clearCache() vs logout({cleanup:true})**: cleanup:true tears down connectors, causing "Wallet connector not ready". Use clearCache() to clear session without destroying SDK state.
    72→- **Window reuse**: Existing window created with on_new_window handler keeps it across show/hide. Don't destroy+recreate (label conflict). Just reload page with eval("location.reload()").
    73→- **OAuth popup cleanup**: Destroy oauth-popup-* windows after auth completes in Rust handle_auth_complete.
    74→
    75→## Staging Environment
    76→- **Branch:** `feat/phase-9.1`
    77→- **VPS:** 76.13.151.200 (Hostinger), SSH key `~/.ssh/ci-deploy-key`
    78→- **API:** https://api-staging.cipherbox.cc (Caddy reverse proxy)
    79→- **Web:** https://app-staging.cipherbox.cc (Caddy static files)
    80→- **Domains:** use flat subdomains (`api-staging`, not `api.staging`) — Cloudflare Universal SSL only covers `*.cipherbox.cc`
    81→- **Deploy:** push tag `v<version>-staging-rc-<N>` (e.g., `v0.1.2-staging-rc-1`) triggers `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml`
    82→- **GitHub Environment:** `staging` — secrets/vars split (vars for non-sensitive, secrets for sensitive)
    83→- **Docker Compose `.env`:** must be named `.env` (not `.env.staging`) for variable substitution
    84→- **pnpm Docker:** use `pnpm deploy --prod --legacy` for flat node_modules without symlinks
    85→- **Cloudflare IPFS gateway deprecated** — serve web app from Caddy instead
    86→- See `.learnings/2026-02-09-staging-deployment-first-deploy.md` for full details
    87→
    88→## Pencil MCP Lessons
    89→- **Changes are in-memory only** — `batch_design` does NOT write to `.pen` file on disk. User must save from Pencil editor. Ask user to save periodically during long sessions to avoid data loss if MCP disconnects.
    90→- **Modal overlay pattern**: Copy base screen, switch container to `layout: "none"`, set x/y/width/height on children explicitly, layer backdrop + modal on top.
    91→- **Copy gives new IDs**: After `C()`, must `batch_get` to discover new descendant IDs. Never `U()` descendants of a just-copied node in the same batch.
    92→- **Multi-screen workflow**: Create all placeholder frames first (`placeholder: true`), then build content, then remove placeholders when done.
    93→- See `.learnings/2026-02-09-pencil-mcp-multi-screen-design.md` for full details.
    94→
    95→## CRITICAL: TypeORM Migration Discipline
    96→- **`synchronize: true` in dev/test hides missing migrations** — tables auto-created from entities, so missing `CREATE TABLE` migrations are invisible until staging/production deploy
    97→- **Every new `@Entity()` MUST have a corresponding `CREATE TABLE` migration** — check `apps/api/src/migrations/` before merging any PR that adds entities
    98→- **FullSchema baseline does NOT need updating** — it's a point-in-time snapshot; incremental migrations with `IF NOT EXISTS` run on both fresh and existing databases
    99→- **Use `IF NOT EXISTS`** for idempotency (pattern: `1740000000000-AddDeviceApprovals.ts`)
   100→- **Migration timestamp ordering matters** — create-table migration must have earlier timestamp than any migration that modifies that table
   101→- Phase 14 hit this exact bug: `SharesPartialUniqueIndex` tried to modify `shares` table that didn't exist on staging. Fixed in PR #186.
   102→- **Full protocol:** `docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md`
   103→
   104→## CRITICAL: Run E2E Tests Locally Before Pushing
   105→- **Always run the relevant E2E test suite locally before pushing** — CI feedback takes minutes, local runs are immediate
   106→- Command: `cd tests/web-e2e && pnpm exec playwright test tests/<feature>.spec.ts`
   107→- Even if tests fail for infra reasons (API not running), the attempt validates test syntax, imports, and structure
   108→- Requires API + frontend running locally for full pass
   109→- See `.learnings/2026-02-24-run-e2e-locally-before-push.md`
   110→
   111→## Conventional Commits & Branch Naming
   112→- **Choose the commit type carefully** — it flows into changelogs and release notes via Release Please
   113→- `fix:` implies a **bug fix** and triggers a **patch version bump** — do not use for config cleanup or maintenance
   114→- `chore:` is correct for non-functional changes (config, tooling, dependency cleanup, removing unused settings)
   115→- `feat:` triggers a **minor version bump** — only use for actual new features
   116→- Branch prefix should match the commit type: `chore/remove-statusline-config`, not `fix/...`
   117→- Misusing `fix:` for non-bugfix changes pollutes the changelog with fake "Bug Fixes" entries
   118→- When in doubt, prefer `chore:` over `fix:` — a missing changelog entry is less harmful than a misleading one
   119→
   120→## Patterns & Lessons
   121→- Always verify state machines end-to-end in UAT, not just individual transitions
   122→- For async deduplication, share the Promise, not a boolean flag
   123→- Never use eventually-consistent external services as sole source of truth
   124→- Playwright UAT catches UX issues that code review and static verification miss
   125→- FUSE-T NFS is much more fragile than kernel FUSE — every inconsistency breaks the mount
   126→- **Zustand stale closures in async callbacks**: React hook selectors (`useFolderStore()`) capture state at render time. Inside `useCallback` async functions, use `useFolderStore.getState()` to read fresh state — especially when effects in other hooks modify the store in the same render commit.
   127→- **`useInterval` doesn't fire immediately**: Need a separate `useEffect` for fire-on-mount behavior.
   128→- **GitHub PR review comments workflow**: See `.learnings/2026-02-10-pr-review-comment-workflow.md` for full details. Key points:
   129→  - Use GraphQL `reviewThreads` query (not REST) to get `isResolved` status per thread
   130→  - Reply via REST: `gh api repos/OWNER/REPO/pulls/N/comments --field in_reply_to=COMMENT_ID --raw-field body='...'` (use `--field` not `-f` for `in_reply_to` — it must be sent as integer, `-f` sends string which causes 422)
   131→  - Resolve via GraphQL: `resolveReviewThread(input: {threadId: "..."})` — can batch multiple in one mutation
   132→  - Always check code state before acting — comments may already be addressed in later commits
   133→  - For already-addressed comments: reply with explanation of where/how it was fixed, then resolve
   134→  - For inapplicable comments: reply with reasoning for not making changes, then resolve
   135→- **Don't justify code duplication with build optimization**: "Vite dead-code eliminates the unused branch" doesn't matter — maintainability and avoiding divergent code paths matters more.
   136→- **GSD UI keyword detection must be kept in sync** across 4 locations: `quick.md:105`, `discuss-phase.md:505`, `design/sync.md` (ui_keyword_heuristic section), `execute-phase.md` workflow (design_sync_check step). The old `gsd-verifier.md:699` location no longer exists. When adding new UI-related keywords, update all four. The list was expanded in Feb 2026 to include interaction keywords (drag, drop, hover, animation, etc.) after DnD task wasn't detected as UI work.
   137→
   138→## Metadata Schema Documentation
   139→- **Schema reference:** `docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md` -- documents all 10 metadata objects (FolderMetadata, FileMetadata, DeviceRegistry, etc.) with field tables, encryption, storage, source file cross-references
   140→- **Evolution protocol:** `docs/METADATA_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md` -- formal rules for evolving metadata schemas (additive vs breaking changes, version bump rules, dual-platform checklist)
   141→- **ALWAYS consult these docs** before any ticket that adds/removes/changes fields on metadata objects
   142→- Evolution checklist (Section 4 of protocol) must be completed for every schema change
   143→- Key rule: optional fields with sensible defaults = no version bump; removing/changing fields = version bump required
   144→
```

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"fix(ci): change staging tag format to avoid release-please collision\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- Changes staging tag format from `v{version}-staging-rc-{N}` to `staging-v{version}-rc-{N}`\n- Adds `packages/crypto` to release-please config for independent semver versioning\n\n## Root Cause\n\nRelease-please scans all GitHub Releases to find the \"latest release\" for each configured package path. The root package (`.`) uses `include-component-in-tag: false`, so its tag pattern is `v{version}`. Staging pre-release tags like `v0.26.0-staging-rc-1` matched this pattern, causing release-please to either match them to path `.` (wrong version) or skip the actual `v0.26.0` release entirely. This resulted in \"Missing 1 paths: .\" followed by \"There are untagged, merged release PRs outstanding - aborting\".\n\nThe `staging-` prefix ensures staging tags never collide with `v{version}`.\n\n## Cleanup performed\n\n- Deleted 4 old-format staging GitHub Releases (`v0.26.2-staging-rc-1`, `v0.26.0-staging-rc-1`, `v0.25.2-staging-rc-1`, `v0.25.1-staging-rc-1`) and their tags\n- Manually created `v0.26.1` release to unblock release-please\n- Flipped PR #323 label from `autorelease: pending` to `autorelease: tagged`\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [ ] Merge this PR\n- [ ] Verify release-please creates next release PR correctly (no \"untagged merged release PRs\" error)\n- [ ] Deploy to staging using new tag format: `staging-v{version}-rc-1`\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Create PR"
}
```

> AGENT

Good news and bad news:

**Good news**: Release-please actually **worked** this time — it successfully created PR #327 as the next release PR. The staging tag collision fix resolved the root deadlock. It found `v0.26.1` for path `.` and proceeded.

**Bad news**: It failed at the very last step trying to add a label to the PR:

```
release-please failed: Validation Failed: {"resource":"Label","code":"unprocessable","field":"data",
"message":"Could not resolve to a node with the global id of 'PR_kwDOQ6DXvM7MjpGj'."}
```

This is a GitHub API race condition — the PR was just created (PR #327) and the label API call fired before GitHub's internal graph had resolved the PR node. This is a transient error. Re-running the workflow should fix it, or we can just manually add the `autorelease: pending` label to PR #327.

We should create an anchor release for it too, same as we did for the root. That way release-please only picks up changes since that point:

> AGENT

Found the problem. Someguy is crash-looping:

```
Restarting (1) 20 seconds ago
could not parse "1GB" as uint64 value from environment variable "SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY" for flag libp2p-max-memory
```

`SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY` expects a raw byte count (uint64), not a human-readable string like `1GB`. It worked locally because our local Docker host might be running a different someguy version or the env var was being ignored. Let me fix this.

> TOOL

```json
{
  "command": "# 1GB = 1073741824 bytes\necho $((1024 * 1024 * 1024))",
  "description": "Calculate 1GB in bytes"
}
```

> AGENT

Two problems:

1. **`staging-v0.26.2-rc-1`** release is being parsed as component `staging` — the `staging-` prefix made the tag not collide with `v{version}`, but the GitHub Release created by Tauri's publish step is still visible to release-please's release scanner, which tries to parse ALL releases.

2. **`v0.26.1`** exists but isn't being matched to path `.` — this might be because the release body doesn't match what release-please expects, or the `targetCommitish` is a raw SHA instead of `main`.

The staging release format needs to not create a GitHub Release at all, or use a completely different naming scheme that release-please can't parse as a component. But that's a Tauri action behavior we can't easily control.

The more immediate fix: the deploy-staging workflow's Tauri step creates the GitHub Release. We could skip creating a GitHub Release for staging desktop builds, or we need to make release-please ignore certain releases. 

Want me to delete the `staging-v0.26.2-rc-1` release and re-trigger release-please to see if `v0.26.1` gets found without it, or should we step back and think about the architecture here?

> AGENT

Line 286 is the key:

```
✔ No latest release found for path: ., component: , but a previous version (0.26.3) was specified in the manifest.
```

Release-please scans all GitHub Releases and **cannot match `v0.26.2` to path `.`**. The component for path `.` is empty string (`component: `). When `include-component-in-tag: false`, release-please looks for tags matching `v{version}` with NO component prefix. But it still needs to match the release to the path, and it's failing to do so.

Let me check what the `v0.26.2` release that release-please itself created looks like vs what it's looking for.

> AGENT

It finds all 5 sub-packages but never `v0.26.2` for `.`. This is bizarre — `v0.26.2` was created by release-please itself, has `include-component-in-tag: false`, tag is `v0.26.2`, name is `v0.26.2`. 

I think the issue is that the root package's `component` field is being derived from `package.json`'s `name` field as `cipher-box`, and release-please is looking for a release with component `cipher-box` in the tag — but the tag is just `v0.26.2` with no component. There's a mismatch between what release-please creates and what it searches for.

> AGENT

Good point — but the problem is that with `include-component-in-tag: false`, release-please creates tags as `v0.26.2` (no component in tag). If we set component to `cipher-box`, it still creates `v0.26.2` tags, but when scanning it looks for a release matching component `cipher-box` — and `v0.26.2` has no component, so it doesn't match.

The mismatch is between:
- **Tag creation**: `include-component-in-tag: false` → `v{version}`
- **Tag scanning**: looks for component `cipher-box` in the release name/tag

Setting component to empty string makes both sides consistent: create `v{version}`, scan for `v{version}` with no component.

Alternatively, we could set `include-component-in-tag: true` and use `cipher-box` as the component — but then all root tags would become `cipher-box-v0.26.2` instead of `v0.26.2`, which changes the tag format for all existing and future releases. That would also break the `tag-staging-release` workflow which looks for `v*` tags.

When scanning releases to find the latest for path `.`, it looks for component `cipher-box` and can't match bare `v{version}` tags — resulting in \"Missing 1 paths: .\" and the \"untagged merged release PRs outstanding\" deadlock.\n\nSetting `\"component\": \"\"` explicitly aligns the release scanner with the tag format.\n\nAlso cleaned up 29 old `v*-staging-rc-*` GitHub Releases that were polluting the release list.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [ ] Merge → release-please creates release PR without \"Missing paths\" warning\n- [ ] Merge release PR → `v0.26.4` tag created automatically\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
```json
  "description": "Create PR"
}
```

**Still** "Missing 1 paths: .". The `component: ""` fix didn't help. Even with an empty component, release-please cannot match `v0.26.2` to path `.`.

This is a release-please bug. It creates `v0.26.2` with `include-component-in-tag: false` but its own release scanner can't find it. The release exists, the tag exists, the format is correct, but the matching logic fails.

At this point I think the pragmatic fix is to close PR #331, manually create `v0.26.3` (for PR #329's merge), flip its label to tagged, and then re-run release-please so it creates a clean PR with only the changes since `v0.26.3`. The `component` fix may help future runs find `v0.26.3` — or we may need to file a release-please bug.

> AGENT

Still "Missing 1 paths: .". The `component: ""` fix didn't change anything — release-please still can't match `v0.26.3` to path `.`. The issue is deeper than the component name.

The `v0.26.3` release was created manually by us. The `v0.26.2` release was created by release-please. Neither is being found for path `.`. This means there's something fundamentally wrong with how the release scanner matches bare `v{version}` tags to the root package when other packages exist.

I think we need to accept that `include-component-in-tag: false` is broken in release-please's multi-package mode and switch to `include-component-in-tag: true` with component `cipher-box`. That means tags become `cipher-box-v0.26.4` but at least they'll actually work. Want to try that approach?

It creates bare `v{version}` tags but cannot match them back to path `.` during release discovery, causing persistent \"Missing 1 paths: .\" errors and the \"untagged merged release PRs outstanding\" deadlock.\n\n## Changes\n\n- `release-please-config.json`: `component: \"cipher-box\"`, `include-component-in-tag: true`\n- `tag-staging.yml`: updated description to reflect new tag format\n- `release-gate.yml`: looks for `cipher-box-v*` tags with fallback to legacy `v*`\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [ ] Merge → release-please creates PR with `cipher-box-v0.26.5` tag reference\n- [ ] Merge release PR → `cipher-box-v0.26.5` tag created automatically\n- [ ] Run `tag-staging-release` with `cipher-box-v0.26.5`\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",

Once this merges, release-please should find `cipher-box-v0.26.4` for path `.` and create a clean release PR with `cipher-box-v0.26.5`. Then we can finally deploy to staging.

> DEVELOPER

Is this new setup not going to clash with our new staging tag name format of staging-v-

> AGENT

No, they're fine together. Release-please parses tags as `{component}-v{version}`:

- `cipher-box-v0.26.5` → component `cipher-box` → matches root package
- `staging-v0.26.5-rc-1` → component `staging` → not configured, ignored with a warning

We already saw that warning in the logs earlier (`Found release tag with component 'staging', but not configured in manifest`) and it didn't cause any issues — it just logs and moves on.

> AGENT

Here's the full staging results summary:

**Someguy sidecar**: Up and healthy (8 hours, actively connecting to DHT peers)

**SDK E2E (83 tests)**: All pass, zero errors

**IPNS Publish Storm (5 clients x 50 cycles = 750 ops)**:
| Metric | Local (someguy) | Staging (someguy) |
|---|---|---|
| Throughput | 8.39 ops/s | **15.28 ops/s** |
| createFolder p50 | 728ms | **468ms** |
| deleteItem p50 | 469ms | **246ms** |
| renameItem p50 | 520ms | **182ms** |
| Errors | 0 | 0 |

**Mixed Workload (5 clients x 45 mixed ops)**:
| Metric | Local (someguy) | Staging (someguy) |
|---|---|---|
| Throughput | 3.68 ops/s | **9.32 ops/s** |
| uploadFile p50 | 1.5s | **613ms** |
| Errors | 0 | 0 |

Staging is significantly faster than local — the VPS has a warmer DHT routing table (8 hours uptime) and better network proximity to IPFS peers.

PR #335 wires the throttle bypass into future deploys so this doesn't get lost.

> AGENT

From the earlier local tests:

**IPNS Publish Storm (5 clients x 50 cycles = 750 ops)**:

| Metric | Staging (someguy) | Local (delegated-ipfs.dev) | Local (someguy) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Throughput | **15.28 ops/s** | 10.75 ops/s | 8.39 ops/s |
| createFolder p50 | **468ms** | 606ms | 728ms |
| createFolder p99 | **1.0s** | 1.62s | 1.27s |
| deleteItem p50 | **246ms** | 395ms | 469ms |
| renameItem p50 | **182ms** | 305ms | 520ms |
| Errors | **0** | **0** | **0** |

**Mixed Workload (5 clients x 45 mixed ops)**:

| Metric | Staging (someguy) | Local (delegated-ipfs.dev) | Local (someguy) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Throughput | **9.32 ops/s** | 5.50 ops/s | 3.68 ops/s |
| uploadFile p50 | **613ms** | 1.1s | 1.5s |
| Errors | **0** | **0** | **0** |

Staging someguy beats delegated-ipfs.dev on every metric — median latency roughly 40-50% lower across the board, nearly double the throughput. The warm DHT routing table makes all the difference. The earlier local comparison was misleading because our sidecar had a cold DHT.
